Prohibited leaps: Using general correlation, unconditional coupling, or omissions at the same scale to support G4; combining G4a and G4b after the fact; changing success criteria within the same subtype after the fact or using unselected criteria to rescue the claim; directly calling a result that is not statistically significant or does not pass the positive threshold single-scale closure, absence of object conversion, or equivalence of interventions; treating a wider scale range as a truer explanation or greater authorization; treating a shared term as proof of object or intervention identity.

Method checks: E4; E5; EVIDENCE

G4a separately tests conditional increments from excluded scale variables. G4b tests only its preselected item among noncommutation of object dynamics, noncommutation of intervention, failure of the pre-frozen K, change in effective relations, or change in intervention response. The G4-instance cited by C6 must have frozen both one subtype and one success criterion before results; switching is prohibited after results, whether between subtypes or within one subtype.

### 4.12.4 C7 Local Improvement and Cross-Boundary Spillover

Inferential dependencies: G2; claims that actually cross scales additionally require a D3/E5 mapping, and claims of closure failure or object/intervention conversion further require the corresponding G4 instance.

Additional conditions: The local target, object boundary, window of impact, and variables outside the boundary have been registered. A G2-instance locates the cross-boundary channel, with temporal order, a mechanism bridge, and a blocking or substitution counterfactual between the local change and the outcome outside the boundary. Crossing an object boundary or an administrative boundary does not automatically constitute a change of scale; D3/E5 is checked separately when scales are actually crossed. The corresponding G4 is tested separately only when closure failure, object conversion, or intervention conversion is additionally claimed. Using “externality” requires stating the costs and benefits and who bears or receives them. These must be specified within the institutional or accounting arrangements at issue.

Permitted conclusions: Cross-boundary spillover accompanying local improvement, together with its distribution across positions, may be registered when the mechanism bridge and counterfactual hold; it is called an externality only when an additional institutional or accounting definition is available.

Prohibited leaps: Claiming that every local improvement necessarily harms a larger scale; directly calling cross-boundary correlation a spillover mechanism; calling something an externality without an institutional or accounting definition; automatically assigning compensation, responsibility, or an action plan upon finding a spillover.

Method checks: E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE; additionally E5 when scales are actually crossed.

First measure the predetermined improvement in the local target, then trace outputs, risks, resource occupation, or signals outside the boundary along the channel specified by G2, using proportionate comparisons to distinguish a common shock from a cross-boundary effect. When making a cross-scale statement, specify the D3/E5 mapping; when stating scale-closure failure or object conversion, provide the corresponding G4 evidence. A model with sound closure can also represent cross-boundary effects correctly; inadequacy of a model must not be made a necessary condition for the existence of an effect. The institutional or accounting definition further determines whether the costs and benefits at issue are externalities.

| Contract | Minimum Evidence | Key Counterexamples | Failure Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C4 | The historical-increment branch requires G3 and control of the current representation; the carrier route obtains evidence under §4.5; the repair branch requires K*/F*, timing comparisons, and the corresponding causal basis | Carrier formation or subsequent effects have not been distinguished; the residual is absorbed by a sufficient current state; intervention timing makes no stable difference | Absence of G3 blocks only additional historical increments; without causal evidence, do not call it historical causation; without K*/F* and a timing basis, do not call it a repair window |
| C5 | G2-instance; demand and capacity of the same type or a conversion mapping; measurement by position within the same window; carrier accumulation requires formation, retention, recovery, and causal comparison across windows; the historical-increment kind additionally has G3 | Capacity expands at the same time; demand and capacity are incomparable; no subsequent difference remains after the shortfall is removed; sustained common inputs explain the outcome | When type, unit, window, or channel is insufficient, describe only a limited resource state; insufficient evidence across windows does not establish an accumulation mechanism; absence of G3 blocks only the historical-increment branch |
| C6 | Complete D3/E5, preselected G4 subtype and criterion, competing explanations, and the corresponding review; a positive conclusion requires a record that the preselected criterion passed; a null conclusion requires records of passing the corresponding equivalence or sufficiency, statistical power or sensitivity, and tolerance requirements; the two routes are established separately, neither being a premise for the other | Retained variables form a sufficient statistic; the gain comes from omissions at the same scale or from complexity; M commutes with dynamics/intervention and the mapping is stable; insufficient sample or measurement sensitivity prevents the positive-support check from passing but cannot support the null either | Return to the source scale if the contract is incomplete, the subtype or criterion was not preselected, an unselected criterion is used for rescue, or the result is not reproduced; remain unresolved if neither the positive-support check nor the null-conclusion rule passes |
| C7 | Local variables and variables outside the boundary; a channel specified by G2, temporal sequence, and counterfactual; D3/E5 additionally for a cross-scale claim; G4 additionally for a claim of closure failure or object conversion; an institutional/accounting definition additionally for an externality | Blocking the channel has no effect; a common shock explains the difference; the claimed cost does not fall within that accounting definition | Without cross-boundary causation, register only correlation or an accounting gap; closure holding does not negate spillover; without the corresponding definition of where costs and benefits fall, no externality classification is obtained |

Crossing boundaries, crossing scales, and model closure answer different questions. Transmission at the same scale can have real effects. So can cost shifting across administrative boundaries. Synchronized changes caused by a common cause do not become cross-boundary effects through correlation alone. Existing domain evidence is retained according to its research design. When using C7's formal mechanism route, check the channel, mapping, and accounting conditions actually required by the claimed level. 〔Studies 04.05, 20.07, 26.02, 28.02〕

## 4.13 Group 3: Patterns of Evolution, Observation, Human Uptake, and Institutional Write-Back

### 4.13.1 C8 Variation–Differential Retention–Reproduction

Inferential dependencies: D1

Additional conditions: V: distinguishable variations and their sources are defined before results; D: differences in outcomes exceeding a threshold occur in comparable environments; R: those differences enter the next round and can be reviewed across repeated rounds; random drift, common external causes, sampling bias, and one-time shocks serve as competing explanations; a claim about a specific carrying mechanism additionally requires a G2-instance.

Permitted conclusions: When V, D, R, entry into the next round, repeated rounds, and comparison with random drift as a competing explanation all hold, a variation–differential retention–reproduction pattern may be registered within the specified environment; a specific carrying mechanism is registered only with an additional G2-instance.

Prohibited leaps: Explaining every instance of evolution through selection; equating a single instance of persistence or a single sample difference with differential retention; treating what is retained as better, more advanced, or more justified; equating systemic selection with a subject's choice, collective governance, or authorization.

Method checks: E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE

C8 is a contract for testing a pattern that requires independent evidence; it cannot be proved directly from a definition or the framework's narrative. The basic-pattern branch requires V, D, R, entry into the next round, repeated rounds, and comparison with random drift as a competing explanation to hold together. The specific-mechanism branch additionally requires a G2-instance to locate the retention or reproduction channel. It describes systemic selection, not an acting subject's intentional choice, still less authorization for governance.

The branches are registered as `C8-pattern` (V, D, R, the next round, repeated rounds, and comparison with random drift as a competing explanation) and `C8-mechanism` (additionally G2, with a specified retention or reproduction channel and channel perturbation or natural variation).

### 4.13.2 C9 Observation Channels and Reflexive Responses

Inferential dependencies: G2

Additional conditions: Observation, naming, scoring, or publication reaches the object through an actual specified channel; the object's response has temporal order and a counterfactual involving no observation, a different observation, or a blocked channel. The carrier route for a lasting response must check how the response enters the current state, how it is retained or updated across windows, and its sustained effects under §4.5, distinguishing sustained observation, repeated stimulation, and common changes. Historical-increment lasting reflexivity additionally requires a G3-instance.

Permitted conclusions: Observational participation or a reflexive response may be registered within a specified channel and window. When the conditions for continuation through a carrier hold, a reflexive response maintained through the current carrier may be registered within that window. With an additional G3-instance, historical-increment lasting reflexivity beyond the current state may be registered.

Prohibited leaps: Claiming that all observation changes its object; equating a reaction after observation with the object's original essence; treating reflexivity as automatic authorization to conceal or suppress observation, or to act on the object.

Method checks: E2; E3; E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE

The derivation first freezes the observation position, measurement protocol, and existing blind spots, then uses a G2-instance to identify the observation event's specified propagation channel, establishing a counterfactual through no observation, alternative observation, or channel blockage. A single reaction supports only a reflexive response within the corresponding window. Continuation across windows requires separate checks of the carrier, updates, and evidence of subsequent effects under §4.5; repeated stimulation must not be treated as retention of a single impact. The historical-increment lasting branch is entered only when G3 shows a historical increment across windows beyond the current state.

The branches are registered as `C9-response` (an actual channel and counterfactual), `C9-carried-persistence` (additionally a current carrier, continuation or updating across windows, and causal comparison under §4.5), and `C9-persistent` (the historical-increment kind; additionally G3, with a historical conditional increment and review across windows).

### 4.13.3 C10 Human Uptake and Intertemporal Reproduction

Inferential dependencies: G2

Additional conditions: The object is a structure involving human action or care. A structure involving maintenance or institutional execution also qualifies. H2 separately registers uptake carriers, bearers of costs of the same type, beneficiaries, rights to stop, and responsible parties. The carrier-reproduction route must check the retention, replenishment, replacement, and actual continuation of capabilities, rules, resources, or relations over multiple cycles under §4.5, with G2 channel evidence covering the corresponding outcomes and windows; the historical-increment intertemporal branch additionally requires a G3-instance. A claim about historical traces in the H5 sense additionally requires an H5 instance; traces alone do not establish reproduction.

Permitted conclusions: The basic branch may register the human uptake chain and its current distribution. When the carrier route's conditions hold, candidate intertemporal uptake or reproduction may be registered within specified cycles and carriers. With an additional G3, candidate historical-increment intertemporal reproduction beyond the current state may be registered. With an additional H5 instance, historical traces may be delimited within its scope of support.

Prohibited leaps: Equating capability for uptake with an obligation of uptake; equating the most visible executor with the responsible party; treating H2 classification itself as proof of a reproduction mechanism; directly authorizing a particular subject's sacrifice, continuation, or exit from the existence of an uptake chain.

Method checks: H2; E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE

C10 specializes to structures involving human action, care, maintenance, or institutional execution. The current-uptake branch uses G2 to locate a specified channel and separates uptake carriers, bearers of costs of the same type, beneficiaries, rights to stop, and responsible parties under H2. The intertemporal carrier branch must separately check continuation across consecutive cycles and its causal evidence; the historical-increment intertemporal branch additionally requires G3. H5 supports only historical traces within its contract's scope and cannot replace evidence of effects for either intertemporal route. H2 is an operational check, not an empirical cause.

The branches are registered as `C10-current` (H2 typing and a channel specified by G2), `C10-carried-reproduction` (additionally intertemporal carriers, replenishment or replacement, actual continuation, and causal evidence under §4.5), `C10-intertemporal` (the historical-increment kind; additionally G3, with an intertemporal historical increment), and `C10-historical-carrier` (a carrier qualification on the historical-increment branch; additionally G3 and H5, with a human historical-carrier instance).

### 4.13.4 C11 Institutional Write-Back

Inferential dependencies: D2; G2

Additional conditions: The object has institutional fields such as records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, memory, or stopping conditions. H3 serves as a classification check. It requires feedback to cause field changes. It also requires actual execution. Intake, field changes, execution, and duration are recorded separately.

Permitted conclusions: Intake, field changes, actual execution, effective write-back, and its duration within an institution may be distinguished.

Prohibited leaps: Equating the existence of an appeal or audit entry point with effective write-back; equating a single write-back with long-term repair or positive improvement; treating the fact of institutional write-back as proof that the institution is justified or its authorization has expanded.

Method checks: H3; E4; CAUSAL; EVIDENCE

C11 uses G2 to locate an institutional return channel, compares conditions with a return against no return or blockage under D2, then distinguishes intake, field changes, and actual execution under H3. Duration is part of the write-back contract; a single write-back is not the same as learning. `C3-carried-learning` separately checks update retention, invocation over multiple rounds, and task outcomes; the historical-increment branch `C3-learning/CM-LEARNING-history` additionally requires G3, updates that can be retained, and repeated rounds (see §4.5 and §6.3).

| Contract | Minimum Evidence | Key Counterexamples | Failure Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C8 | Sources of V; D in comparable environments; an R channel, the next round, and repeated rounds; random drift/common external causes as competing explanations; G2 additionally for the mechanism branch | Common inputs produce the pattern simultaneously; differences do not enter the next round; random drift suffices; perturbing the reproduction channel has no effect | If any of V/D/R/next round/repeated rounds is missing, register only change or a single difference; without G2, do not call it a specific mechanism |
| C9 | Observation position, propagation channel, G2-instance, temporal sequence, and a no-observation/alternative-observation/blocking counterfactual; continuation through a carrier additionally has retention, updating, and comparison across windows; the historical-increment lasting branch additionally has G3 | No change after the channel is blocked; changes precede observation; sustained stimulation or common inputs explain subsequent changes | Without a channel or counterfactual, do not register observational participation; without evidence across windows, do not extrapolate continuation; absence of G3 blocks only the historical-increment lasting branch |
| C10 | G2-instance; H2 typing and a cost mapping for quantities of the same type; comparisons of replenishment/rotation/load reduction/exit/replacement; intertemporal carriers additionally have evidence of continuation across consecutive cycles; the historical-increment intertemporal branch additionally has G3, and the trace qualification additionally has H5 | In the carrier branch, continuation does not occur or is explained by common inputs; in the historical-increment branch, the current state absorbs all historical differences | If H2 fields cannot be separated, register only a data gap; insufficient intertemporal evidence does not establish a reproduction mechanism; absence of G3 blocks only the historical-increment intertemporal branch; absence of H5 prevents its formal trace eligibility |
| C11 | G2-instance; institutional return channel and a blocking counterfactual; changes in fields before and after, with actual execution; duration | Only a receipt or a statement of position; field changes are not executed; independent orders explain the changes | Without field changes, describe only intake; without execution, describe only record updating; intertemporal mechanisms must not be extrapolated from a single write-back |

## 4.14 Group 4: Normative Bridging Checks

### 4.14.1 C12 Check on the Validity of Normative Bridging

Inferential dependencies: N1; O1; O2; O3; O4

Additional conditions: O1 has frozen an auditable description, evidential status, and the action ceiling supported by the description. O2 registers `explicit_normative_premise_ids`, positive targets or constraint properties, conflicts, and dissent at runtime. O3 registers the authorizing subject, source, jurisdiction, validity period, J axis, candidate options, and the non-action option. O4 registers constrained execution, monitoring, stopping, appeal, review, rollback, remedy, expiry, and renewed authorization. The protection floor has been checked item by item. N1 alone can only block overreach; it cannot generate a positive option.

Permitted conclusions: Only when the auditable description, explicit N premises at runtime, protection floor, authorization on the J axis, and all of O1–O4 pass may a constrained recommendation eligible for real-world adoption be formed. Forming an analytical recommendation for deliberation requires explicit normative premises, evidence, and the price to be paid, but does not require authorization already to have been obtained for real-world execution that has not yet occurred. If only N1 is present or positive N is missing, the only permitted outputs are non-action, obtaining further evidence, or continued deliberation.

Prohibited leaps: Inferring an “ought” directly from facts about stability, efficiency, persistence, coherence, paths, or selection; treating expert explanation, model confidence, wide impact, or legal validity, each on its own, as normative justification; inferring love, responsibility, a requirement of sacrifice, or authority to act from G1–G4, C1–C11, or S0–S6; filling in an option's target with normative principles that have not been explicitly cited.

Method checks: EVIDENCE; PF-1; PF-2; PF-3; PF-4; PF-5; PF-6; PF-7; PF-8; PF-9; PF-10

C12 is not an empirical inferred conclusion, but a check on procedural validity before description enters real-world choice. It requires all 10 bridging components to appear: `descriptive_or_explanatory_claim_ids`, `current_evidence_and_epistemic_constraints`, `explicit_normative_premise_ids`, `normative_conflict_record`, `protection_floor_status`, `authorization_and_jurisdiction_record`, `options_including_no_action`, `operational_procedure_records`, `action_ceiling`, and `stop_review_rollback_and_remedy`. N1 is a negative check against overreach; it cannot produce a positive option on its own.

| Minimum Evidence | Key Counterexamples | Failure Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| O1 description freezing and evidence audit; O2 explicit N premises and conflicts; O3 authorization and comparison of options; O4 constrained execution and correction; protection floor and J axis | The same facts lead to different choices under different N; only N1 is present without a positive target; authorization does not cover the action; stopping, correction, or rollback is inaccessible | Real-world action must not begin if any required explicit N premise is missing. The same applies if the protection floor, J-axis authorization, or the O1–O4 record is missing. If only N1 is present, retain the negative check against overreach. |

## 4.15 Combined Conditions and Evidence Routes

The relation between root instances and additional conditions can be condensed into the following condition trees:

G1-instance + object/variable bridge + G2-instance
  └─ Candidate channel on which the specified form of organization depends
      ├─ + Return channel and blocking counterfactual
      │    └─ Effective feedback
      │         ├─ + Current update carrier + invocation over multiple rounds and task comparison (§4.5)
      │         │    └─ Candidate carrier-transmission feedback-mediated learning
      │         └─ + G3-instance + updates that can be retained + repeated rounds
      │              └─ Candidate historical-increment feedback-mediated learning
      └─ + G4a/G4b + D3/E5 mapping
           └─ Candidate closure failure or object/intervention conversion

G2-instance + mapping of demand/capacity of the same type
  └─ Candidate instantaneous overload
       ├─ + Current carrier + accumulation/recovery across windows and causal comparison (§4.5)
       │    └─ Candidate carrier-transmission cumulative damage or delayed recovery
       └─ + G3-instance + historical carrier
            └─ Candidate historical-increment cumulative damage or delayed recovery beyond the current state

D1 + V + D + R + next round + repeated rounds + comparison with random drift as a competing explanation
  └─ Variation–differential retention–reproduction pattern
       ├─ + G2-instance → specified retention/reproduction mechanism
       └─ + G3-instance → candidate historical path across rounds

Listing the names of G instances alone does not constitute sufficient conditions for a mechanism; the corresponding additional conditions, method checks, bridging evidence, and limits of application must also hold. When conditions are complete, register limited results under the specific contract. When conditions are incomplete, identify the gap and the conclusion it blocks without withdrawing independently supported content. Even if every instance in a particular set of G1–G4 instances passes, this does not imply an S0–S6 stage, direction, value, responsibility, choice, or authorization; these conclusions each require their own classification, normative, and procedural contracts.

## 4.16 Scope and Stopping Points of Empirical Judgments

Empirical judgments provide descriptions and limited explanations constrained by the object, scale, time window, evidence, and extrapolation unit. Specific factual assertions within G question families must be tested through instances; C registers existing results or combines mechanism conditions; F provides proofs under formal premises; and real-world mechanisms still require independent instance evidence. Definitions, identifiers, examples, and internal descriptions cannot determine whether an empirical proposition holds in the world. Further evaluation of value, responsibility, sacrifice, choice, or how the situation should be handled requires adding human-domain typing, normative reasons, and O1–O4 procedures. Empirical conclusions must not bypass these responsibilities on their own.

## 4.17 Common Method Constraints

Method constraints govern how analysts construct, check, and use models; they do not claim how the world necessarily works. They may be questioned and revised, and their use must be evaluated through comparisons of actual reductions in error, gains in understanding, and costs of use.

### 4.17.1 CMC1 Tasks and Representations Must Be Declared Together

Objects, layering, coarse and fine scales, and target variables are tied to the task, observation position, horizon, environment, and use. A conversion must check what is retained, collapsed, omitted, and added. It must not be presumed lossless, nor may it be claimed conversely that every conversion necessarily causes a task-relevant loss. Whether the target representation suffices to recover an answer, advance dynamics, or evaluate admissible interventions is tested separately; whether different orders of conversion are equivalent is also tested separately. The distinctions on which the task depends must be retained. Recovering every detail unconditionally is unnecessary.

### 4.17.2 CMC2 The Status of Materials and Scope of Support Must Be Traceable

Reports, observations, model beliefs, and formal proofs retain their respective statuses. So do simulation outputs and execution receipts. Each inference specifies the new information, premises, and rules it uses. Processing the same data again must not be counted as a new independent observation. A simulated state can become the starting point of the next simulation, but recursive depth alone cannot add empirical support or raise a capability level. New computations or proofs can improve understanding of existing materials, but their gains come from inspectable arguments and must not be packaged as new real-world samples.

### 4.17.3 CMC3 Facts, Values, and Eligibility to Act Are Established Separately

Facts, predictions, formal feasibility, and technical capability cannot by themselves generate value, responsibility, or authority to act. Analytical recommendations disclose normative targets, evidence, the price to be paid, and dissent. Real-world adoption additionally requires valid permissions, a protection floor, implementation resources, and conditions for stopping and remedy. Normative premises constrain choice; they do not in turn prove empirical mechanisms true.

### 4.17.4 Method Effectiveness and Evaluation of the Framework

G question families organize empirical testing; their number does not establish sufficient coverage. C contracts organize the use of results; their completeness does not establish real-world reliability. Testing whether the entire framework is worth using should involve a separate comparison with an adequate specialist method or a simpler approach, using common targets, materials, and budgets. The comparison should ask whether the framework reduces substantive errors, whether it preserves necessary distinctions, and whether it improves explanation or decision-making. It should also assess whether the costs are proportionate. Without such a comparison, only the intended uses and checked local results may be stated.

The effect of an individual instance, a question family's rate of applicability within a particular set of objects, and the framework's net gain on a specified task are three different empirical targets. Sampling units, scopes of extrapolation, and evaluation rules must each be registered; a batch of successful instances or a large volume of reading materials must not be treated as proof of universal effectiveness.

## 4.18 Conditional Formal Propositions

The following propositions hold within explicit mathematical objects. They provide checkable conditions for representation, conversion, and action, not unmeasured laws of reality. Real-world application must additionally check variable mappings, assumptions, errors, and scope of application under §2.1.2. Proving that a function exists does not guarantee that it is computable, available in time, implementable, or authorized for adoption.

### 4.18.1 F1 Task Recovery

Let X be a nonempty state domain, with representation π:X→Y and Y=π(X), and target q:X→V. A decoder g:Y→V using only the representation and satisfying q=g∘π exists if and only if π(x)=π(x′) always implies q(x)=q(x′). When this holds, g is unique on Y. In other words, the same representation cannot merge states for which the current task requires different answers. See Appendix C.6 for the proof and for bounds on scalar recovery error on a finite state domain.

### 4.18.2 F2 Closure under Dynamics and Admissible Interventions

Let F:X→X be deterministic one-step dynamics. A macro-level update F̄ satisfying π∘F=F̄∘π exists if and only if any two source states with the same representation still have the same representation after updating. If this holds throughout the state domain, evolution over any finite number of steps under fixed dynamics commutes. With an admissible action set U, this condition must hold separately for every `F_u`. Check whether each action can be implemented across the relevant source states sharing a representation. Check the different micro-level implementations as well. Sufficiency for static recovery does not automatically satisfy closure under dynamics or intervention; a stochastic model must also compare the complete transition probabilities to target blocks. See Appendix C.8 for the proof.

### 4.18.3 F3 Common Feasible Actions under Information Constraints

For a finite, single-round deterministic task, let the current materials be compatible with a nonempty set of states `X_y`, and let each state x have a set of eligible actions `A_ok(x)` already determined according to the target, protections, resources, and valid permissions. An action based only on the current representation y that qualifies in every compatible state exists if and only if `∩_{x∈X_y}A_ok(x)` is nonempty. Identifying every state is therefore not a universal prerequisite for bounded action, while the availability of a solution in each state separately does not guarantee that the right choice can be made now. See Appendix C.12 for the proof; randomized and sequential strategies must establish their own conditions.

### 4.18.4 F4 Task Preservation through Successive Conversions

Let f:X→Z and h:Z→W, with the first stage satisfying q=g∘f. The composition h∘f can still recover the same final target q if and only if g is constant on every fiber of h restricted to the actual image f(X). Completion of different local tasks at the individual steps cannot replace this shared-endpoint condition. Preservation under a functional representation, causal effects connected in sequence, and joint implementation using shared resources are checked separately; a single flag marking a connection cannot substitute for these different checks. See Appendix C.7 and C.12 for the proof and counterexamples.

### 4.18.5 F5 Error Propagation under Approximate Closure

Let the target space have a metric d. Assume that the one-step projection error is no greater than ε at every state reached, and that the Lipschitz constant of the proposed macro-level update on the relevant region is no greater than L. Starting from the same initial representation, the error after t steps satisfies `e_t≤ε∑_{j=0}^{t−1}L^j`. This bound requires the error and region conditions to continue to hold at every step; mean fitting error cannot directly replace a state-by-state bound. When L<1, there is an upper bound ε/(1−L); when L=1, it is tε; when L>1, the upper bound may increase rapidly. See Appendix C.8 for the proof.

## 4.19 Task Families and Dynamic Refinement

Which distinctions a structural representation should retain depends on the tasks at issue and the operations allowed to act on the object. A finer representation can preserve more differences and also increases measurement, storage, explanation, and maintenance costs; not every detail is a necessary input to every task. In a fully specified finite deterministic model, it is possible to state precisely which states can be merged and compute the coarsest partition satisfying particular requirements.

Let X be a nonempty finite state set, Q a finite set of target functions `q:X→V_q`, and U a finite set of admissible actions. Each u∈U corresponds to a total deterministic update `F_u:X→X`; the actions are implementable in every state, and no unregistered external disturbance is introduced here. The empty action string denotes no update. For an action string `w=u₁…u_k`, write `F_w=F_{u_k}∘…∘F_{u₁}`. These conditions delimit the objects of the proposition below; they cannot be assumed satisfied merely because real-world operations share a name.

**F6 The Coarsest Sufficient Partition for a Task Family.** For a nonnegative integer H, define `x∼_H x′` if and only if, for every q∈Q and every admissible action string w of length no greater than H, `q(F_w(x))=q(F_w(x′))`. This is an equivalence relation. The representation formed by its equivalence classes retains precisely the task answers within the given horizon. Any deterministic representation that answers these questions exactly may merge only states within the same equivalence class.

Proof: Reflexivity, symmetry, and transitivity follow from the corresponding properties of equality of function values. The answer for every target and action string is constant within an equivalence class, so by F1 it is uniquely determined by that class. If another representation merges two nonequivalent states, then by definition there must be a target and an admissible action string giving different answers; by F1, that representation cannot recover both answers exactly. Thus, the partition induced by any sufficient representation is finer than or equal to this partition. This coarseness concerns a state partition relative to X, Q, U, and H; it does not establish the fewest parameters, the cheapest measurement, or the irreplaceability of a particular natural-language concept.

Sufficiency for a fixed finite horizon does not automatically produce dynamic closure for that same horizon. Take X={a,b,c,d}, with the sole target q(a)=q(b)=q(c)=0 and q(d)=1, and the sole action satisfying F(a)=c, F(b)=b, F(c)=d, and F(d)=d. States a and b both give 0 now and after one step, so a∼₁b. Their successors c and b, however, give different answers after one further step, so c and b do not belong to the same ∼₁ class. For H≥1, what is always guaranteed is that `x∼_H x′` implies `F_u(x)∼_{H−1}F_u(x′)`. The remaining horizon should be reduced when advancing, or closure of the fixed partition under admissible actions must be required separately.

To preserve every target under admissible action strings of any finite length, define `x∼_*x′` to hold when the answers agree for all such finite strings. This partition is closed under each `F_u`. Prepending u to any subsequent string gives a string already compared in the definition. Conversely, any representation that recovers all targets in Q and is closed under all `F_u` can be advanced step by step and used to recover the targets after any finite string, so the partition it induces must be finer than or equal to `∼_*`. Thus `∼_*` is the unique coarsest state partition satisfying these conditions. Uniqueness is determined by the equivalence classes, irrespective of their labels.

On a finite domain, this partition can be computed directly. Begin by grouping states by their values on all current targets to form P₀. Each round uses only the preceding partition `P_h` and splits its blocks synchronously by the signature `([x]_{P_h},([F_u(x)]_{P_h})_{u∈U})` to obtain `P_{h+1}`. Here `[x]_{P_h}` denotes the block containing x in the preceding partition. Each strict refinement adds at least one block, so at most `|X|−1` strict refinements can occur. Once no block splits further, the blocks preserve the current targets and satisfy closure under each action. Level H of synchronous iteration corresponds exactly to target comparisons for strings of length at most H, so the stable partition equals `∼_*`. With asynchronous refinement one block at a time, final stability can still be checked. Its operation count must not be used as the horizon H here. This procedure is computable on a complete finite table. It does not guarantee identification of true states from incomplete observations, much less tractability for large models within a given budget.

This result gives a conditional rule for simplification. Merge states that cannot be distinguished by the given targets and admissible operations. Retain a distinction only when a task or an operation actually distinguishes the states. Adding targets or admissible actions leaves the required partition unchanged or refines it. Shortening the horizon, narrowing the use, or restricting the operations can make a simpler representation sufficient. Adjusting the task can yield a legitimate new question, but the adjustment does not warrant declaring the original task complete.

Error tolerances require separate analysis. The condition “the two answers differ by no more than ε” is generally not transitive. The values 0 and ε are close, and ε and 2ε are close, but 0 and 2ε may differ by more than the tolerance. Approximate similarity therefore cannot directly replace the equivalence relation above or automatically yield a unique coarsest partition. One may use a bound on block diameter, worst-case recovery error, or a specified probabilistic loss. The optimal solution, stability, and computational cost for each require separate proof.

This section states a proposition about sets and dynamics in a finite model. Distinguishing states by input strings, forming equivalence classes, and refining them recursively have a direct mathematical connection to Moore’s work on sequential machines. The preservation of states and actions also connects to abstraction in controlled dynamics. The explicit finite model and proof in this section establish the conclusion without relying on additional optimality results for other classes of machines. 〔Moore, 1956, pp. 143 and 145–146; Ravindran and Barto, 2003〕 It neither declares that nature has a single correct hierarchy nor reduces people’s legitimate claims to the current predictive targets. Which targets must be preserved, which actions may be implemented, and what error is acceptable still have to be determined separately on the basis of domain facts, normative reasons, and real-world permissions. When these givens are challenged, they are handled under §2.14.

[^tn-contract-05]: Translator's note: A contract here is an analytical agreement registered and frozen before results, close to pre-registration. It is not a legal instrument.

<a id="part_5"></a>

# Part 5 Cross-Scale and Cross-Circle Transformations

When an object, observation, or action moves from SP0 to SP1, this Part asks what expands, what contracts, which positions are incomparable, what justifies a conversion bridge, and what information the conversion loses. The general process is called scale transformation. The nine axes are compared using a product partial order. The term scale elevation applies only when the relation in that order is strict.

## 5.1 Contract[^tn-contract-06] Roles and Unambiguous References

A scale axis is one of nine definitions of kind D with the role `scale_axis_definition`. The nine conversion operators are procedures of kind O with the role `scale_transformation_operator`. U01–U11 are definitions of kind D for universal primitives and have the role `universal_primitive_definition`. Definitions and procedures make no claim to empirical truth. Specific claims of cross-layer causation must link to a pre-registered `root-instance`. The same requirement applies to object conversion. A null conclusion must also link to such an instance.

Always use qualified IDs for scale entities, from `scale_axis:A` to `scale_axis:J`, from `scale_operator:M01` to `scale_operator:M09`, and from `universal_primitive:U01` to `universal_primitive:U11`. The identifier `scale_axis:O` denotes organizational level. The expression `claim_type=O` denotes an operational procedure. The only dependencies are `inferential_requires`, `protocol_requires`, `specializes`, and `applies_to`. Protocols[^tn-protocol-06] use formal IDs such as `CAUSAL`, `EVIDENCE`, `ANALOGY`, and `SOURCE`. They must not use Chinese abbreviations or bare axis names.

## 5.2 Nine-Axis States and Axis-by-Axis Comparison

Each object and transformation is bound to the following:

SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>

| Axis | Core State Fields | Computational Witness for `expands` | Limits on Substitution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A Aggregation Level | Units, membership set, partition, aggregation rule, weights, and exclusions | The target population covers the source population, and the target partition is a registered coarsening of the source partition | O, X, I, and J must not substitute for A |
| X Spatial Extent | Coordinate system, spatial set, boundary channels, and external connections | Once the coordinates are aligned, the source space is a proper subset of the target space | A, O, I, and J must not substitute for X |
| T Time Span | Time reference, window role, start and end points, and time-lag model | With the same reference and role, the target interval properly contains the source interval | A current cross-section and a long-term path cannot substitute for each other |
| O Organizational Level | Organizational graph, version, nodes, containment edges, interfaces, and overlaps | In the same version of the organizational DAG, the target node covers the ancestor closure of the source node | A higher position in the organization does not amount to an expansion of J |
| C Causal Level | Causal model, variables, edges, intervention semantics, and abstraction mapping | The target model covers the source model through a semantics-preserving mapping and adds distinguishable levels | Level labels, temporal order, and correlation cannot substitute for a causal bridge |
| R Observation Resolution | Measurement protocol, distinguishable classes, parameters, error, and protective omission | The target protocol preserves every distinction in the source protocol and subdivides at least one class | High resolution does not imply completeness or authority to act |
| I Scope of Influence | Outcome, threshold, window, affected positions, and effect order | After alignment, the target set of affected positions properly contains the source set | Neither influence nor observation amounts to authorization |
| N Network Topology Scope | Graph and version, nodes, edges, semantics, and sampling boundary | A semantics-preserving graph embedding exists, and the target graph covers the source graph | Network centrality alone does not identify a center of responsibility |
| J Scope of Jurisdiction and Authorization | Set of atomic authorization tuples, each specifying its source, subject, single object, single action, geographic area, validity period, withdrawal, validity, and evidence | The normalized set of valid target tuples properly contains the source set, and each added tuple has an independent witness to its validity | No other axis can substitute for J. Taking a Cartesian product of the object and action sets is prohibited |

An axis can have only five relations: `equal`, `expands`, `contracts`, `incomparable`, and `unknown`. A comparison must preserve the source and target states, the rule and version used, the actual mappings or set differences, information loss, and uncertainty. It must allow others to review the comparison. Expansion or contraction must not be registered between identical states. On the same axis, `contracts` is the inverse of `expands`. A witness for a nontrivial relation must come from a comparison in the relevant domain. The record cannot supply such a witness by declaring itself “valid.” Without a basis that can be reviewed, the relation remains `unknown`.

All nine axes must be registered. Whether an axis applies depends on the object. If an entire axis genuinely does not apply, use a `not_applicable` object with a reason for the axis state and keep the axis in the record. A typical example is a natural process with neither an acting subject nor a concept of authorization. J does not apply to that process, which differs from having an empty set of authorizations. If the axis is inapplicable on both the source and target sides under the same applicability criterion, the witness may mark it `equal`. If it is inapplicable on one side and applicable on the other, the semantic domain has changed and the relation should be marked `incomparable`. If the material is insufficient, it remains `unknown`. This keeps the nine-axis interface stable and avoids imposing the semantics of human institutions on the world broadly conceived.

Comparison on each axis must satisfy reflexivity, antisymmetry, and transitivity. To keep “almost equal” from breaking transitivity, first use `equality_rule` to normalize axis states into equivalence classes, then define the partial order on the quotient set of those classes. A tolerance may be used only when a pre-registered shared normalizer or fixed binning places both states in the same equivalence class. A rule that directly declares two arbitrary points equal when their “distance is below the threshold” must not be used. Reflexivity requires a normalized state to be `equal` to itself. Antisymmetry requires that if each of two states is less than or equal to the other, both belong to the same equivalence class. Transitivity holds only when the intermediate state, versions, and witnesses can be composed. Any break in the mappings, coordinates, graph versions, variables, or authorization chain returns the relation to `unknown`.

The primary quantity compared on an axis does not suffice by itself. If the axis record includes auxiliary states such as boundary channels, time-lag models, interfaces, error models, intervention semantics, or protective omissions, `expands` also requires composable, semantics-preserving mappings for these fields. If the primary extent increases and the auxiliary semantics conflict with no mapping between them, the relation may only be recorded as `incomparable` or `unknown`. This prevents a “wider extent” from passing as a “higher state on the entire axis.”

## 5.3 Product Partial Order and Transformation Classification

SP0≼SP1 if and only if all nine axes are `equal` or `expands`. The strict relation SP0≺SP1 additionally requires at least one axis to be `expands`. The machine applies the classification rules in this order:

If any axis is `incomparable`, classify as `horizontal_or_incomparable`. An `unknown` relation on another axis does not override known incomparability.

Otherwise, if there is both expansion and contraction, classify as `mixed`. Two known relations in opposite directions rule out ordering under the product partial order, even if some axes remain `unknown`.

Otherwise, if any axis is `unknown`, classify as `unresolved`.

If all nine axes are `equal`, classify as `all_equal`.

If all axes are `equal` or `expands`, with at least one expansion, classify as `elevation`.

If all axes are `equal` or `contracts`, with at least one contraction, classify as `reduction`.

Thus, macro-level aggregation that also compresses resolution is typically `mixed` and should not be called elevation across the board. Analogies between domains are often `horizontal_or_incomparable`. With insufficient material, the classification is `unresolved`. Only instances classified as `elevation` may be called “scale elevation.” An increase in the scope of observation, space, organization, influence, or network leaves J unchanged. An expansion of J can be witnessed only by new, valid authorization tuples.

If a J state stored “one source + a set of subjects + a set of objects + a set of actions” as independent fields, it would generate object–action combinations that had never been authorized. The `authorization_tuple_contract` requires each atomic tuple to bind exactly one source, one decision-making subject, one object, and one action and to preserve the geographic area, validity period, withdrawal conditions, evidence, and independent review. Multiple objects or actions must be split into multiple tuples. Only normalized atomic tuples with a valid status enter the set comparison. The witness for J-axis expansion must list, in `comparison_payload`, the complete tuples added on the target side and absent from the source side, along with independent evidence of their validity. It must align with `j_authorization` in the record. Arbitrary strings, tuple IDs, self-assertions, actual control, observation coverage, increased influence, a higher position in the organization, and expansions on other axes cannot make J `expands`.

## 5.4 Operator Outcome Statuses and the Null-Conclusion Check

The `operator_ids` of each atomic record must contain exactly one operator. Then `selected_operator_branch` selects exactly one internal branch from that operator’s branch registry. Finally, `claim_mode` declares the mode of this evaluation as `descriptive_mapping` (descriptive mapping), `root_hypothesis` (an instance of a family of empirical questions), `causal` (causal bridge), `object_conversion` (object conversion), or `intervention_conversion` (intervention conversion). The branch registry specifies which modes each branch allows. The three fields must be compatible and must be frozen before the results are read. Failure at the positive-support check must not lead to a switch of branch or a fallback to a looser descriptive mode. Material from one mode must not be used to rescue another. If an actual process contains multiple operators, it must be broken into an ordered chain of atomic records. The target object and SP1 at each step must align with the source object and SP0 at the next step. Each step must register its own outcome status, evidence, loss, and error. A single overall result cannot close the individual steps.

All operator procedures use the same four outcome statuses:

`supported` means the bridge, decision rule, and positive threshold for the selected branch all underwent pre-registration and have all been satisfied. A `root-instance`, a preselected subtype, and a single success criterion are mandatory only for G, causal, object-conversion, and intervention-conversion branches. A purely descriptive branch may mark those G-specific fields `not_applicable`.

`unsupported_or_undecided` means bridging is insufficient, or the positive-support check and the null-conclusion check have both failed to pass.

`null_supported` supports a limited null conclusion only when the pre-registered `null_decision_rule` and the equivalence or sufficiency test both pass. The rule may require statistical power. It may instead require sensitivity. The applicable requirement and the tolerance must also pass.

`not_evaluated` means the operator or the relevant branch has yet to run. A branch that has run and established its descriptive bridge can be `supported`. It must not be downgraded to “not evaluated” merely because it requires no G-instance.

“The result was not statistically significant,” “the outcome appeared unchanged after cutting the link,” “the effect disappeared,” “the simple model performed comparably,” and “the evidence for the target was insufficient” do not by themselves warrant `null_supported`. This rule applies to every operator from M01 to M09, including the ones concerned with cross-layer causation.

## 5.5 The Nine Scale Transformation Operators

Each operator has a distinct `semantic_signature`. The aggregation statement for M01 must not be copied and passed off as that of another operator.

### 5.5.1 M01 Aggregation

The signature is “unit-to-population partition aggregation.” At minimum, the bridge must cover a mapping for each unit, membership and exclusions, weights and missingness, alternative aggregation rules, and heterogeneity. Information loss includes distribution tails, order, covariances, local temporal sequences, and the visibility of minority positions. The procedure fails if the aggregate cannot be reproduced under the registered rules, if a reasonable alternative rule reverses the direction, or if an aggregate association is imputed to individuals as an attribute. Even when aggregation is supported, the aggregate result must not be used to take direct action concerning members.

An aggregation comparison first fixes the estimand. Average improvement among a region’s current residents and improvement in a fixed cohort of pre-disaster residents are two different questions. People who moved away, died, could not attend, or did not apply must not be silently assigned zero need. Where needed, show the fixed cohort, the current cross-section, entrants, and those who exit side by side. A crude rate and a standardized rate can both be correct. Only if the rates are claimed to answer the same question but disagree should the measurement definitions, selection, or errors of inference be examined further. 〔Studies 03.04–03.05, 22.07, 24.01, 29.04, 31.04〕

### 5.5.2 M02 Nesting

The signature is “boundary–membership embedding.” It must be divided into two branches:

Descriptive nesting tests only boundaries, membership, overlap, exit, and interface mappings. It can hold without any cross-layer causation.

Cross-layer causation requires a specified channel, a comparison suited to the claim, and temporal order. Evidence of empirical effects must be obtained under G2 and `CAUSAL`. The cross-scale mapping must separately go through D3/E5. A corresponding G4 instance is required only for a further claim of scale closure failure or of object or intervention conversion. Cross-layer causation can be established without a positive G4 result.

The absence of a conditional increment after controlling for the current state and the common environment does not by itself establish “no cross-layer effect.” A limited null conclusion may be registered only if the equivalence or sufficiency, statistical power or sensitivity, and tolerance checks all pass. Descriptive nesting creates no higher-level priority, lower-level obligation, or expansion of J.

A record must select one branch from `descriptive_nesting`, `cross_layer_causal`, `object_conversion`, and `intervention_conversion` and bind it to the corresponding descriptive, causal, object conversion, or intervention conversion mode. Boundary material from descriptive nesting must not be used to support the other three branches.

### 5.5.3 M03 Network Propagation

The signature is “transmission along paths with timing.” The nodes, edges, directions, weights, capacity, and sampling boundary must be registered. The record must also include candidate and alternative paths, delay, and loss. Connection or synchronization yields only candidates. If the outcome is unchanged after the path is cut and the null-conclusion check has not passed, the status remains `unsupported_or_undecided`. Network sampling can miss weak edges, off-network positions, and bridges between networks. Retaining only endpoint impacts cannot recover the order of propagation. Centrality does not amount to intent, responsibility, or authority to act.

Observe tie strength, bridge position, centrality, and mobilizable resources separately. Four contacts can come from four institutions yet share the same information source. An acquaintance can also offer an actual referral that others are unwilling to make. Network position helps locate channels. Their capacity, cost, substitutability, and arrival time determine whether they are actually usable for the specified task. Testing for multiple sources of social support differs from testing for independence among sources of factual information. 〔Studies 26.01–26.02〕

### 5.5.4 M04 Accumulation Over Time

The signature is “longitudinal composition within a window.” The time reference, baseline, window, time lag, durability threshold, and accumulation, decay, and recovery rules must be frozen. The comparisons must address common trends, seasonality, cohorts, and alternative windows. An effect that disappears after controls are applied or changes with the window indicates only insufficient positive support. A finding of “no accumulation” must not be published unless the null-conclusion check passes. Basic temporal composition does not establish G3 in advance. A `G3-instance` is linked only if historical terms still provide a conditional increment after controlling for the current state.

### 5.5.5 M05 Institutionalization

The signature is “durable institutional write-back.” It separates five judgments about an institution. The first is whether it exists in fact. The other judgments are whether it is legally valid, whether it is normatively justified, whether protection is in place, and whether it should continue. Durable changes in records, roles, resources, decision rules, or subsequent transitions can support the fact of institutionalization. Invalid authorization, inadequate participation, failed protection mechanisms, and a lack of appeal, anti-retaliation, or rollback mechanisms constitute governance failures, normative disputes, and grounds for downgrading action. These shortcomings cannot erase the fact that the institution already exists. Conversely, its existence does not establish its legal validity, normative justification, adequacy of protection, or warrant for continuation.

`selected_operator_branch` allows only `institutional_fact`, `institutional_causal_effect`, `institutional_object_conversion`, and `institutional_intervention_conversion`. A legal validity review, governance quality, normative justification, adequacy of protection, or a judgment about whether to continue cannot serve as a positive or null conclusion for `institutional_fact`.

### 5.5.6 M06 Emergence

The signature is “interaction generates a pattern at the target scale.” The source units, interaction rules, target object, and target pattern must be compared with a pre-registered simple additive model using the same metric. Comparable performance by the simple additive model supports “additivity is sufficient” only if the sufficiency or equivalence, statistical power or sensitivity, and tolerance checks pass. Otherwise, the result remains unresolved. A unique micro-level cause cannot be inferred from the macro-level pattern, which does not by itself establish downward causation. A downward constraint requires a specified channel, causal evidence suited to the claim, and a D3/E5 mapping. Additional claims about scale closure or the conversion of an object or intervention require separate evidence under G4.

### 5.5.7 M07 Delegation/Representation

The signature is “classification of facts about representation and optional transfer of authorization.” The claim to represent, actual acts in another’s stead, contested or self-appointed representation, valid delegation, and transfer of J-axis authority must be recorded separately. Even without authorization, the acts performed, their impacts, and the associated responsibility may still be recorded. Expansion of J must not be registered in that case. Invalid authorization triggers a stop or a downgrade. It cannot erase the acts already performed or their consequences. A majority, visibility, influence, self-assertion, or actual control does not amount to valid delegation.

The record must select one branch from `representation_claim`, `actual_acts`, `delegation_validity`, and `J_transfer`. Even if one of the first three branches is supported, it cannot change J. J may expand only if `J_transfer`, structured authorization tuples, independent evidence of validity, and `j_authorization` all pass.

### 5.5.8 M08 Compression/Abstraction

The signature is “many-to-one representational compression.” The source material, algorithm, threshold, version, error, irrecoverable information, and invariants required for the task must be traceable. Keep `unknown`, `not_applicable`, `not_observable`, and `withheld_for_protection` distinct. None may be compressed into “absent.” A representation with material task-relevant losses for a proposed high-impact action must not be used to support that action.

A precise condition for task sufficiency illustrates compression. Let x be the source state, z=f(x) its representation, and q(x) the answer currently required. If and only if f(x₁)=f(x₂) always implies q(x₁)=q(x₂), there exists a function g such that q=g∘f. The original state may be irrecoverable even when the current question can still be answered exactly. This condition gives no guarantee that g is easy to compute or that the representation remains sufficient for a different question, environment, or intervention. 〔Studies 05.01–05.02, 05.05–05.06, formal illustration here〕

Consider a finite, nonempty state domain and a real-valued target q. If the decoder can output any real number and loss is measured by the worst-case absolute error over the whole domain, the minimum recovery error from the representation is exactly half the largest oscillation of the target within a preimage. Let `a_z` and `b_z` denote the minimum and maximum target values in each nonempty preimage. The optimal error is (1/2)max_z(b_z−a_z), attained by outputting the midpoint for each preimage. Given a tolerance ε, there exists a decoder with error at most ε if and only if the oscillation within each preimage is at most 2ε. The formula cannot be applied directly to vector or integer outputs, average loss, or an unknown distribution.

Successive conversions must preserve the same final query. Let f:X→Z and h:Z→W. The first stage is sufficient for the final target q, with q=g∘f. The final representation h∘f remains sufficient if and only if g is constant on every preimage under h restricted to the actual image f(X). Unreachable intermediate values need not be constrained. Completing a different local task at the second stage cannot resolve the original final query. See Appendix C.6–C.7 for the condition and its proof.

### 5.5.9 M09 Horizontal Transfer

The signature is “analogical transfer across domains.” The mappings, differences, discontinuities, prohibited mappings, chain of responsibility in the target domain, and differences on the J axis must be registered. Material from the source domain only generates target candidates. Both `supported` and `null_supported` must come from independent target instances. If target evidence is insufficient, the status is `not_evaluated` or `unsupported_or_undecided`. This insufficiency does not establish the absence of the target mechanism. An analogy creates no authorization to act in the target domain.

## 5.6 Limits of Universal Primitives Across Scales

Universal primitives are interfaces of kind D. They no longer carry empirical null models or falsification templates. Four limits in particular must be fixed:

Basic feedback under U07 requires only a return channel and an update to a subsequent state. It does not require G3. Feedback-mediated learning also requires corresponding evidence of an update carrier, retention across rounds, actual use, and task outcomes. G3 is added only when the claim is a historical conditional increment beyond the currently registered state (see §4.5 and §6.3).

An instantaneous demand–capacity gap under U09 does not require G3. Cumulative damage or delayed recovery requires separate verification of the current carrier of damage or capacity, how that carrier formed, and subsequent recovery. G3 applies only when a claim about a historical increment beyond the current representation is added.

U10 registers only candidate traces, their carriers, and their retention windows. It does not establish path dependence in advance. G3 must still demonstrate a conditional increment from historical terms after controlling for the current state.

The basic phase pattern under U11 requires neither G3 nor G4. Test causal triggering separately under G2. For continuation or delayed recovery sustained by the current carrier, obtain evidence under §4.5. Test hysteresis of the kind that adds a historical increment beyond the current representation separately under G3. Test object conversion separately under G4.

Likewise, U01 only registers candidate objects and does not guarantee G1 in advance. The definition of a relation under U05 does not establish causation. The channel fields under U06 do not establish a channel effect.

## 5.7 Unified Scale Transformation Record

Every instance must include all 14 sections and all fields.

| Section | Required Fields |
| --- | --- |
| `identity` | `contract_id`, `concept_id`, `version`, `proposition_ids`, and `purpose` |
| `scale` | SP0, SP1, nine `axis_differences`, `unchanged_axes`, `transformation_class`, and `j_authorization` |
| `objects` | Valid source and target objects, `source_K`, `target_K`, `identity_mapping`, units, population, boundaries, members, and exclusions |
| `semantics` | `preserved_core`, `allowed_changes`, `lost_elements`, and `prohibited_mappings` |
| `transformation` | One operator, `selected_operator_branch`, `claim_mode`, rule, causal bridge, time lag, mapping error, validity period, `root-instance`, subtype, success criterion, positive and null decision rules, positive threshold, equivalence or sufficiency test, statistical power or sensitivity, tolerance, and outcome status |
| `variables` | Inputs, states, outputs, and dependencies between variables |
| `evidence` | Source and target evidence, coverage, heterogeneity, counterexamples, absence signals, alternative explanations, residual tests, and replication or external validation |
| `loss` | Compressed details, irrecoverable information, positions with low visibility, and local exclusion zones |
| `responsibility` | Actors, decision makers, authorizing parties, uptake carriers, responsible parties, beneficiaries, and cost bearers |
| `normative` | Value premises, choice type, normative choice record, runtime N principles, source of authorization, the C12 check, and O1–O4 procedures |
| `protection` | Protection applicability, low-power positions, safe submission, and anti-retaliation |
| `action` | Judgment ceiling, action ceiling, prohibited actions, stopping conditions, responsible person, and a machine-resolvable `selected_action` |
| `correction` | Appeal, review, rollback, repair, and write-back |
| `lifecycle` | Validity period, review points, suspension, and exit |

Together, `source_K`, `target_K`, and `identity_mapping` determine whether the object is preserved, converted, or incomparable. A shared name supplies no evidence of identity. The mapping record must preserve the summaries of the source and target K, the results of the four checks on the source and target objects under both sets of K, the forward and reverse mappings, the preserved and violated criteria, the pre-registration reference frozen before the results were read, and reviewable validation artifacts and hashes. Criterion results may only be `passed`, `failed`, or `undetermined`. A single Boolean value must not obscure which check did not pass.

| K Mapping Classification | Minimum Conditions | Outcome Ceiling |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `same_object` | The mappings are valid in both directions. All four checks on the source and target objects under `source_K` and `target_K` pass. The set of preserved criteria is nonempty, and the set of violated criteria is empty | The object identity may be retained under the current contract. This does not imply that its semantic, causal, or normative properties are also preserved |
| `converted_object` | `source_under_source_K` and `target_under_target_K` pass, and `target_under_source_K` fails. The violations and the reference to pre-registration before the results are read are specific. The record also uses `object_conversion` mode and has a supported G4b instance | Register only the object conversion under the pre-registered K. K must not be rewritten after the results to manufacture a conversion |
| `incomparable` | Both objects pass under their own K, and at least one cross-K check fails. The forward and reverse mappings preserve a complete record of attempts, with at least one validation showing invalidity and with evidence that can be resolved | Records known incomparability. The only permitted status is `unsupported_or_undecided`, distinct from “not evaluated” |
| `undetermined` | The necessary mappings, criteria, or evidence are still insufficient to complete the classification. Preserve the known `passed` and `failed` results and the `undetermined` results separately for the four checks, and identify which one blocks the overall judgment | Only `unsupported_or_undecided` or `not_evaluated` is permitted. Preserve the partial findings, and do not infer incomparability or object conversion from an unresolved overall judgment |

Object identity must be open to independent recomputation or review. The same object under the same criteria can be checked directly. Nontrivial preservation or conversion requires retaining both objects, both sets of K, mapping direction, the four criterion results, and the supporting materials. A name, ID, self-reported validity status, or hash cannot substitute for this semantic verification.

In object conversion mode, the classification must agree with the four outcome statuses. Only `supported` corresponds to `converted_object`. `null_supported` corresponds to `same_object` when all three null-conclusion checks pass. `unsupported_or_undecided` permits only preservation, known incomparability, or an unresolved classification. When the object conversion test has yet to run, the overall classification remains `undetermined`, and any local criterion results already verified independently are retained. An unrun or failed test therefore cannot justify preemptively recording the target as “already converted.” Other `claim_mode` values must not also report `converted_object` in the same atomic record. Object conversion must have its own atomic test.

When `claim_mode` is `root_hypothesis`, `causal`, `object_conversion`, or `intervention_conversion`, `root_instance_ids` must be filled in. The last three modes also require a nonempty causal bridge. Only `descriptive_mapping` may mark `root-instance`, subtype, and success criterion `not_applicable`. To record `supported` or `null_supported` in a non-descriptive mode, submit a resolvable `root-instance` registry to the validator and check the instance ID, root family, contract version, preselected subtype, single success criterion, and instance status. An apparently correct string prefix cannot stand in for an instance. A descriptive bridge may be recorded as `supported` if it passes the positive-support check. Record `not_evaluated` only when the selected mode has yet to run.

The 14 sections provide a stable interface without projecting human governance semantics onto all objects. `protection.applicability` must explicitly record the object type, downstream use, rationale, and evidence references. It must not be inferred from empty lists such as `actors`. If the object type is `nonhuman` and the uses are limited to `description_only` or to `nonhuman_intervention_without_human_or_sentient_effect` with evidence of no effect on humans or sentient subjects, `safe_submission` and `anti_retaliation` may be marked `not_applicable` with reasons. Real-world experimental or engineering interventions still have an acting subject and an object. Specific J, a person responsible for the action, stopping provisions, review, and rollback must be entered. Only purely descriptive use may mark J as inapplicable. Energy, matter, or computational resources in a natural system do not by themselves trigger this human protection contract. When the object is human, sentient nonhuman, mixed, or unknown, or the use concerns the evaluation of people, the allocation of their scarce resources, their rights, their exposure to risk, or real-world action involving them, the protection fields immediately become mandatory. An earlier inapplicability status must not carry over. The inapplicability of protection never implies that action is normatively justified. Normative choice still requires a separate judgment.

`action_owner` specifies only who is responsible for carrying out or stopping an action. It provides no basis for inferring `claim_mode=intervention_conversion` or establishing that the action is effective or justified. Every external action must link to a selection record through `selection_record_id` and disclose `value_premises` and the runtime `normative_principle_ids`. N1 alone can only block actions that exceed authorization. It cannot generate an affirmative proposal for action. The `feasible_alternatives` in the selection record must be structured options with stable `option_id` values. For each option, freeze `option_kind`, action type, target object, geographic area, validity period, reversibility, expected impacts, and distributional impacts. There must be exactly one option with `option_kind=no_action`, uniquely referenced by `no_action_option_id`. `selected_action.selected_option_id` must resolve to an `external_action` option. The selected action must match that option field by field on action, object, geographic area, and validity period. Selection of a no-action ID, matching only a natural-language label, and rewriting both the action and J without changing the approved option must all be rejected. At least one currently valid, independently reviewed atomic J authorization tuple must cover that subject, object, action, geographic area, and validity period together, and it must agree with the source of authorization. An unrelated authorization cannot back the proposed action even if it is formally valid. The record must also include a passed 10-component `c12_gate`, O1–O4 `procedure_ids`, a person responsible for the action, prohibited actions, stopping provisions, protection, appeal, review, and rollback. If any item is missing, the action ceiling is limited to taking no action or continuing deliberation. Empirical intervention conversion is judged separately by the relevant `root-instance`.

The record chain supplies only the order of composition. Support does not automatically carry from one step to the next. Chain validation must ensure that each step has a unique `contract_id` and exactly one operator, that adjacent object references and identity criteria are compatible, and that each SP1 matches the next SP0. If any step is unresolved, becomes invalid, or exceeds its loss limit, this must be retained in the output for the chain. Success at the final step cannot override it.

Evaluation of the chain must also specify whether the final task is consistent with the tasks at the individual steps and whether shared resources have been committed more than once. Two tasks can each be feasible with one unit of capacity and yet be infeasible together if they share that unit in the same period. If time limits, resources, or permitted strategies are legitimately changed, the new joint option should be evaluated. Representational sufficiency, joint causal response, and joint resource feasibility each have their own criteria. Passing one cannot substitute for passing the other two.

## 5.8 Machine Validation and the Protection Floor

Machine validation checks the consistency of fields, references, versions, and outcome statuses and verifies that the recorded axis relations have been combined correctly. It cannot replace domain comparators in judgments about geometry, organizational graphs, causal abstraction, network embedding, object identity, or authorization validity. Without substantive comparisons and independent materials about the objects, a complete record or algorithmic output cannot justify registering scale elevation. Whatever schema or registry the implementation uses, it must meet the same requirements for traceability and review.

The four missingness statuses form a closed vocabulary. `unknown` means currently unknown. `not_applicable` means genuinely not applicable under the contract. `not_observable` means unobservable through the current channel and within the current window. `withheld_for_protection` means withheld for protection. None means “absent,” and deleting a field cannot substitute for a status.

`carrying_vehicles` and `responsible_subjects` must be recorded in separate fields. Uptake, visibility, being affected, and greater capability do not by themselves create responsibility. Whenever there is an effect on the rights of humans or sentient subjects, on the allocation of their scarce resources, on their exposure to risk, or on how they are treated in practice, the protection floor must identify low-power positions and local exclusion zones. It must provide safe submission, anti-retaliation, stopping provisions, appeal, review, and rollback. In these cases, it must not be marked not applicable. Insufficient protection lowers both the judgment ceiling and the action ceiling. Downstream actions cannot inherit conditional inapplicability from purely descriptive records. A change of use requires reopening the protection review.

## 5.9 Evidence Boundaries and Independent Support

The names, IDs, semantic signatures, and classifications of M01–M09 specify only how a transformation should be described and tested. They provide no empirical support that the transformation occurred, the mapping is valid, or the target property holds. Internal concept labels, examples, field completeness, and results reported by the algorithm itself must not be entered into `source_evidence` or `target_evidence` in place of materials about the objects.

Every actual transformation must obtain independent, resolvable materials separately for the source and target objects. It must specify the mapping, what is preserved, changed, or lost, the error, and the competing explanations, and undergo the same outcome classification and the same positive-support and null-conclusion checks. Source-domain materials can only generate candidates for the target. Without independent support on the target side, the conclusion remains a mapping awaiting testing or unknown.

## 5.10 Cross-Circle Relation Transformation

Scale transformation and cross-circle relation transformation must be kept separate. Scale transformation asks which variables are preserved, changed, or lost for the same question between SP0 and SP1.
