Whether the outcome difference under the comparison conditions exceeds the predetermined threshold |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Resource adjustments<br>2. Sequences of action<br>3. Commitment and exit records<br>4. Evidence of conflict |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Positions of participation. 2. Safety of expression. 3. Data on resources and actions |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Generative events. 2. Mobilization of uptake. 3. Agendas for normative choice |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Distinguish short-term slogans, long-term commitments, and intergenerational memory |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record silence, coerced agreement, and internal heterogeneity |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Aggregation must not erase different goals at positions where people have limited safety |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. People who identify with the anchor<br>2. Dissenters<br>3. People represented<br>4. Cost bearers |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Narratives<br>2. Commitments<br>3. Shared memory<br>4. Institutional goals<br>5. Problem definitions |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Those making claims of representativeness. 2. Those allocating resources on that basis |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | Existence of an anchor does not establish that it is normatively justified |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | Limit R0 and R1 separately. R0 only describes candidate expressions of meaning. When the corresponding H1-instance supports the effect on the selected outcome, R1 may explain a conditional anchor within a limited scope. Do not extrapolate to unselected outcomes |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of candidate anchors, diverse expressions, comparison results, and needs for more evidence. It does not authorize unifying meaning, representing others’ wishes, or coordinating action. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. A repeated slogan does not change any allocation of resources or action<br>2. Uniform expressions under high pressure conceal genuine goals that conflict |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, members can contest representativeness, submit different goals, or refuse to be anchored through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original chain of judgment about the anchor |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline actually revoke the naming of the anchor, remove its force as a representation and its downstream effect on coordination, and restore the state of diverse expression. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

## A.4 HV04 Generative Node (Full Interface Card)

A. Identity, Proposition, and Scope

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Interface ID (`id`) | HV04 |
| Qualified ID (`qualified_id`) | `human_variable:HV04` |
| Name (`name`) | Generative Node |
| Claim type (`claim_type`) | H |
| Contract role (`contract_role`) | `human_variable_interface` |
| Proposition (`proposition`) | Generation must be separated into generative condition GC, generative subject GS, and generative event GE. Emergent generation can occur without an identifiable subject. |
| Scope (`scope`) | Formation of new actions, organizations, institutions, or state transitions in human structures |
| Conditions for suspension (`pause_condition`) | Suspend the relevant claims about the subject or the type when a condition is personified, an event is treated as a subject, or an entity is classified as a generative subject without grounds. R0 may still register candidate GC, GS, and GE and a status of unidentified subject separately. Failure to identify a subject does not establish that there is none. |

B. Formal Dependencies and Limits on Conclusions

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Inferential dependencies (`inferential_requires`) | None (empty set) |
| Protocol dependencies (`protocol_requires`) | 1. EVIDENCE<br>2. SOURCE |
| Qualification/specialization (`specializes`) | None (empty set) |
| References to applicable objects (`applies_to`) | None (empty set) |
| Conditional support routes (`conditional_support_routes`) | 1. route_id=HV04-R0-generation-typing;<br>claim_level=descriptive_classification;<br>when=The required fields for GC, GS, and GE can be registered separately, and emergence without a subject and the status of an unidentified subject are retained;<br>additional_inferential_requires=None (empty set);<br>additional_protocol_requires=None (empty set);<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate generative condition, candidate generative subject, and candidate generative event separately. None may substitute for another;<br>result_ceiling=Classification by strong type only. The existence of a condition, subject, or event does not establish either of the other two or a causal mechanism of generation.<br>2. route_id=HV04-R1-generation-mechanism;<br>claim_level=mechanism_explanation;<br>when=A pre-registered G2-instance identifies an above-threshold effect of GC, GS, or a channel of emergence without a subject on the specified GE state transition;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate mechanism of generation and its GC, GS, and GE classifications for the specified scale, window, and channel;<br>result_ceiling=Do not personify conditions, infer a subject backward from an event, or infer normative justification, responsibility, or authorization from the fact of generation. |
| Permitted inferences (`allowed_inference`) | 1. Conditional paths of generation. 2. Generation with or without a subject |
| Prohibited leaps (`prohibited_leap`) | 1. Attributing intent to a technology or a crisis<br>2. Assuming a generative subject automatically has continuing authorization |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers units of exposure to conditions, action by subjects, and generative events, with their respective populations of cases, distributions, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers sites of generation, organizational or platform boundaries, areas of diffusion, and cross-domain environments. T=Time span covers the period of accumulation of conditions, the window of the subject’s action, the time of the event, and diffusion lag. O=Organizational level covers roles in action, generative teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers generative events, mechanisms of interaction between subjects and conditions, meso-level structures of generation, institutional arrangements, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers the original conditions, actions, and events, sequences of generation, individual cases, outcome distributions, transition metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers direct participants in generation, people taking up the work, indirectly affected people, second-order consequences, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers propagation of conditions, collaboration among subjects, diffusion of events, and emergent connections. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for identification of generation, initiation of change, commitment of resources, and action concerning diffusion. Register scales separately for GC, GS, and GE |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | A combination of conditions, subjects, and events that forms a detectable state transition |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Separation of GC, GS, and GE as strong types |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. New units and populations<br>2. Relations of representation<br>3. Kinds of responsibility<br>4. External impacts |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Generative subjects, conditions, and events can change with scale |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Stable descriptions without formation of a new state or a claim about generation |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Elevating a condition or an event to a subject with intent |

D. State, Evidence, and Variable Flows

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | 1. Potential<br>2. Triggered<br>3. Forming<br>4. Interrupted<br>5. Diffusing |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Records of when candidate conditions appear, change, or are removed<br>2. The generative subject’s capabilities, authorization, decisions, and actual actions<br>3. The pre-registered state transition from before to after the generative event<br>4. Records of the bridge from local interaction to aggregate outcome in emergence without a subject |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Records of initiation<br>2. Windows for conditions<br>3. Actions by subjects<br>4. Mechanisms of interaction without a subject |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Orientation anchor. 2. Resources and institutional conditions. 3. Causal contract |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Need for uptake. 2. State transitions. 3. The start of a chain of responsibility |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register accumulation of conditions, triggering events, and lags in formation |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record joint generation, emergence without a subject, and subjects that cannot be identified |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Overlooked informal initiators and affected positions |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. Initiators<br>2. People taking up the work<br>3. Beneficiaries<br>4. People affected |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Initiators<br>2. Procedures<br>3. Technical facilities<br>4. Relationship networks |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Those who actually act. 2. Decision makers. 3. Parties granting authorization |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | The fact of generation does not establish normative justification or a complete attribution of responsibility |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | Up to the level of explanation when the mechanism chain is complete |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only candidate classifications for GC, GS, and GE, descriptions of state transitions, and needs for more evidence. It does not authorize starting, spreading, or stopping a generative process. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. A technological condition is incorrectly described as a generative subject with a goal<br>2. An emergent process without a unified initiator is forced into an attribution to one visible person |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people classified as generative subjects can challenge attributions of intent, role, and authorization through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original classification or decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline correct the GC, GS, and GE types and actually remove erroneous attributions of intent or responsibility and their downstream effects. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

| Conditional support routes (`conditional_support_routes`) | 1. route_id=HV05-R0-carrier-responsibility-split;<br>claim_level=descriptive_classification;<br>when=CV, bearers of costs of the same type, beneficiaries, rights to stop, RS, resources, and capacity can be recorded separately under H2;<br>additional_inferential_requires=None (empty set);<br>additional_protocol_requires=None (empty set);<br>allowed_conclusion=Register current uptake carriers, tasks, costs, capacity, rights to stop, types of responsibility, and gaps in uptake;<br>result_ceiling=Only the current classification and description of gaps. The capability to take up work does not become an obligation, and CV does not become RS.<br>2. route_id=HV05-R1-functional-carrier-effect;<br>claim_level=conditional_effect;<br>when=An eligible G2-instance shows that replacing, interrupting, replenishing, or reducing load on the specified carrier has a channel effect above the threshold on the preselected functional outcome;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register the candidate uptake effect of the specified carrier on the measured dimensions of function, capacity, delay, or loss;<br>result_ceiling=Unmeasured dimensions remain unknown. The fact of uptake does not generate a duty or authorization to continue taking up the work. |

| Prohibited leaps (`prohibited_leap`) | 1. Treating the most visible person as primarily responsible<br>2. Inferring that someone ought to take up work because they can<br>3. Assigning responsibility solely because facilities or procedures execute a function. A=Aggregation level covers individual tasks or uptake events, cases involving carriers, the uptake population, distributions of costs and capacity, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers work sites, team or organizational boundaries, digital systems, and the reach of services across domains. T=Time span covers task cycles, maintenance windows, recovery lags, and the period in which responsibility applies. O=Organizational level covers execution roles, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers events of execution, mechanisms of interaction between tasks and resources, meso-level uptake structures, institutional arrangements of responsibility, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers original task logs, sequences of uptake, cases involving carriers, distributions of costs and capacity, performance metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers those directly taking up the work, people dependent on services, positions that indirectly receive benefits or bear costs, second-order spillovers, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers dependencies in uptake, alternative paths, single-point bottlenecks, and service networks across domains. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for task assignment, stopping, resource adjustments, attribution of responsibility, and remedy. Register scales separately for CV and RS |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | People, positions, procedures, facilities, or institutions that actually execute, transmit, maintain, record, provide care, or repair |

| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Aggregation of tasks<br>2. Representation and delegation<br>3. Six kinds of responsibility<br>4. External costs |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Uptake carriers and responsible parties can change with the level |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman processes without action by a subject, responsibility, or maintenance requirements |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Equating uptake by an individual directly with organizational responsibility |

Records of rights to stop, alternative arrangements, and transfer of uptake<br>4. Grounds for decisions, authorization, oversight, benefit, and remedy |

If the objective concerns overall burden, check management, being on call, conditions for those taking over, and continuity separately. A judgment about one task need not collect all of these |

| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record hidden labor, informal care, and costs outside the boundary |

| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Personnel<br>2. Positions<br>3. Procedures<br>4. Facilities<br>5. Institutions |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Parties responsible for conduct<br>2. Parties responsible for decisions<br>3. Parties responsible for authorization<br>4. Parties responsible for oversight<br>5. Parties responsible as beneficiaries<br>6. Parties responsible for remedy |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | The fact of uptake does not generate a duty to continue taking up the work |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | R0 is limited to classification of uptake and responsibility. R1 is limited to identified functional effects of carriers. R3 is limited to durable traces supported by H5. R4 explains reproduction through carriers under §4.5 and with channel evidence covering subsequent windows. R2 explains historical increments beyond the current representation only with additional G3. Each route is limited by its own materials and window. No route may substitute for the others or create an unlimited duty to take up the work. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of CV, RS, costs, capacity, rights to stop, and gaps in uptake, along with needs to reduce load, add resources, or reallocate them. It does not authorize changes in tasks, resource allocation, attribution of responsibility, or protective action. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. The most visible person with limited execution authority is not the party responsible for decisions or authorization, or responsible as a beneficiary<br>2. An automated facility takes up transmission tasks but cannot bear moral or legal responsibility |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people taking up work can challenge tasks, resources, costs, restrictions on the right to stop, and attribution of responsibility through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original chain of task assignment or attribution |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline revoke erroneous task, resource, or responsibility statuses, actually restore previous task and record states, and carry out authorized remedies. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. A compelling vision and concentrated mobilization lack sustained resources, maintenance, or repayment<br>2. A chain that appears connected end to end shifts key costs to people taking up work outside its boundary |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people taking up work along the chain or at affected positions can challenge resources, costs, and attribution to the chain through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original judgment about the chain or the decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline actually revoke attribution to the chain as a whole and cancel the downstream effect of that attribution, and restore descriptions at the level of individual nodes and the unresolved status. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

## A.7 HV07 Feedback Write-Back (Full Interface Card)

A. Identity, Proposition, and Scope

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Interface ID (`id`) | HV07 |
| Qualified ID (`qualified_id`) | `human_variable:HV07` |
| Name (`name`) | Feedback Write-Back |
| Claim type (`claim_type`) | H |
| Contract role (`contract_role`) | `human_variable_interface` |
| Proposition (`proposition`) | An appeal, audit, or piece of feedback constitutes institutional write-back in a human structure only if it changes records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, memory, or stopping conditions and the corresponding change has actually been executed. |
| Scope (`scope`) | Human structures with feedback, appeals, audits, or governance procedures |
| Conditions for suspension (`pause_condition`) | Suspend the claim of institutional write-back if there is no state update and only a receipt, statement of position, or publication, or if field changes have not actually been executed. Limited statuses such as submitted, intake complete, and fields changed are still classified under R0. |

B. Formal Dependencies and Limits on Conclusions

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Inferential dependencies (`inferential_requires`) | 1. D2 |
| Protocol dependencies (`protocol_requires`) | 1. EVIDENCE<br>2. SOURCE |
| Qualification/specialization (`specializes`) | 1. H3 |
| References to applicable objects (`applies_to`) | None (empty set) |
| Conditional support routes (`conditional_support_routes`) | 1. route_id=HV07-R0-writeback-classification;<br>claim_level=descriptive_classification;<br>when=Input channels, receipts, field versions before and after, execution records, time of entry into effect, duration, and stopping or rollback status can be checked separately;<br>additional_inferential_requires=None (empty set);<br>additional_protocol_requires=None (empty set);<br>allowed_conclusion=Distinguish the write-back statuses of not submitted, submitted, intake complete, fields changed, executed, continuing, or no longer in effect;<br>result_ceiling=Only field changes that are actually executed count as institutional write-back. A single write-back does not count as learning, and write-back does not amount to successful correction. Retain the procedural value of upholding a decision for reasons, and any other changes that have occurred, according to their own evidence.<br>2. route_id=HV07-R1-causal-feedback;<br>claim_level=mechanism_explanation;<br>when=An eligible G2-instance shows that an institutional return channel changes the preselected subsequent state or transition relative to a condition without a return or with the channel blocked;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register effective feedback and effects of institutional write-back within the specified fields, channel, and window;<br>result_ceiling=Do not infer learning, long-term repair, normative justification, or broader authorization from the presence of feedback.<br>3. route_id=HV07-R2-feedback-mediated-learning;<br>claim_level=intertemporal_explanation;<br>when=Effective feedback already has support from a G2-instance, and a G3-instance shows that a retainable update provides a historical conditional increment for a predetermined task over repeated rounds;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance, G3-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate for feedback-mediated learning with a historical increment beyond the current state, limited to the specified task, rounds, and window;<br>result_ceiling=Do not claim that the institution as a whole has learned, that repair is complete, or that its direction is normatively right.<br>4. route_id=HV07-R3-carried-learning;<br>claim_level=intertemporal_explanation;<br>when=An eligible G2-instance already supports effective feedback. The current carrier of the update, retention and actual use across rounds, a comparison for the predetermined task, and the causal basis for that comparison are verified under C3-carried-learning and §4.5. An instance of immediate feedback does not automatically cover later task outcomes;<br>additional_inferential_requires=G2-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register a candidate for feedback-mediated learning through carrier transmission within the specified update carrier, task, and rounds;<br>result_ceiling=This does not qualify as a historical increment under CM-LEARNING-history or G3. Do not directly call training, archiving, write-back, or one success learning. Do not claim the institution as a whole has improved. |
| Permitted inferences (`allowed_inference`) | 1. Effective write-back, blocked write-back, and feedback in appearance only |
| Prohibited leaps (`prohibited_leap`) | 1. Inferring learning from the presence of a channel<br>2. Treating one update as long-term repair<br>3. Treating silence as consent |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers individual feedback events or appeals, cases of write-back, the feedback population, distributions of intake and execution, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers submission channels, organizational or platform boundaries, institutional jurisdictions, and the reach of appeals across domains. T=Time span covers submission, intake, field changes, execution, duration, and review lags. O=Organizational level covers feedback roles, intake teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers feedback events, mechanisms of interaction in write-back, meso-level procedural structures, institutional rules, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers original feedback, processing sequences, cases of write-back, outcome distributions, timeliness metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers people directly appealing or taking up the work, indirectly affected people, second-order institutional consequences, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers feedback channels, intake nodes, review paths, blockages, and connections across levels. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for intake, field changes, execution, stopping, rollback, and remedy. Register paths across levels |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | A return channel that changes a subsequent institutional state or transition |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Source and channel of feedback, fields written back, and subsequent changes |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Representativeness of feedback<br>2. Write-back paths across levels<br>3. Aggregation loss<br>4. External review |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Write-back carriers, lags, and responsible parties can change |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Processes without records, rules, resources, roles, or stopping conditions |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Treating feedback from an individual case as directly representative of the population’s views |

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | 1. Arrival<br>2. Intake<br>3. Write-back<br>4. Blockage<br>5. Distortion |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Evidence of submission and intake of feedback or an appeal<br>2. Differences between versions of records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, or stopping conditions<br>3. Execution records, time of entry into effect, and duration of the change<br>4. Review, reversal, remedy, and subsequent state changes |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Original feedback<br>2. Intake trail<br>3. Versions of fields<br>4. Subsequent changes to rules or resources |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Feedback sources<br>2. Safe channels<br>3. Responsible parties<br>4. Review procedures |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Updates to records, rules, resources, roles, responsibilities, memory, or stopping conditions |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register time limits for submission, intake, decision, execution, and further review |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record feedback that did not arrive, anonymity for protection, and handling that cannot be observed |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Positions without safe submission or digital access, or under threat of retaliation |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. People submitting material<br>2. People evaluated<br>3. People carrying out the action<br>4. Beneficiaries of the institution |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Appeal systems<br>2. Audit procedures<br>3. Meeting records<br>4. Repositories of rules<br>5. Chains of responsibility |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Parties responsible for intake<br>2. Decision makers<br>3. Parties who execute write-back<br>4. Parties responsible for oversight |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | Whether feedback is effective and whether its contents are normatively justified are judged separately |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | R0 judges how far the write-back process has actually gone. R1 judges the effect of the return channel with corresponding support from G2. R3 judges feedback-mediated learning through a carrier from retention of the update, its use across rounds, and a task comparison. R2 judges learning with a historical increment only with additional G3. A single write-back, learning in general, transfer, and net benefits are assessed separately. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of intake, field changes, execution, duration, and gaps in write-back, along with needs for review or procedural repair. It does not authorize rewriting records or rules, carrying out repair, or closing appeals. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. An appeal gets a receipt, but records, rules, resources, and stopping conditions all remain unchanged<br>2. An audit report is published without a responsible party, deadline, or subsequent state update |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people who submit feedback can request its status, time limit, responsible party, and write-back outcome through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original intake, write-back, or decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline revoke erroneous updates and actually restore the previous states of records, rules, resources, roles, or stopping conditions. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

Do not infer malice, responsibility, or automatic action from a position or mediation effect.<br>3. route_id=HV08-R2-reflexive-response;<br>claim_level=mechanism_explanation;<br>when=An H4-instance has reflexive response as its single preselected criterion. Observation, naming, scoring, or publication reaches the object through an actual channel, and the instance returns supported;<br>additional_inferential_requires=H4-instance;<br>additional_protocol_requires=E3, CAUSAL, E4;<br>allowed_conclusion=Register reflexive response within the specified observation or publication channel and window;<br>result_ceiling=One response does not count as persistent reflexivity. Do not use the result to conceal observation, suppress expression, or broaden authorization.<br>4. route_id=HV08-R3-carried-reflexive-persistence;<br>claim_level=intertemporal_explanation;<br>when=An H4-instance returns supported on its single preselected reflexive-response criterion within the stated scope across windows. This does not qualify as a historical increment under G3. Do not use the result to conceal observation, suppress expression, or broaden authorization. |
| Permitted inferences (`allowed_inference`) | 1. Conditional opportunities, constraints, masking, and amplification |
| Prohibited leaps (`prohibited_leap`) | 1. Assuming conditions determine individual behavior<br>2. Inferring malice from a position of power<br>3. Attributing intent to an environment |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers individual exposure or evaluation events, cases at particular positions, the population subject to the conditions, distributions of responses, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers sites of interaction, organizational or platform boundaries, public digital spaces, and environments across domains. T=Time span covers windows for accumulation of exposure, response, reflexive change, and fading. O=Organizational level covers roles in action and evaluation, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers exposure events, mechanisms of interaction between conditions and behavior, meso-level power structures, institutional rules, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers original exposures, expressions, and behaviors, time series, cases at particular positions, response distributions, platform metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers people directly evaluated, indirectly affected people, second-order reflexive consequences, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers links of power and information, mediation nodes, areas of masking, amplification paths, and propagation across domains. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for setting rules, using metrics, public evaluation, human review, and action. Compare differences across positions |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | R0 registers a set of potentially relevant conditions. R1 and R2 judge their effects on the preselected outcomes based on the corresponding evidence. The term “potential field” presupposes no potential function, common unit, or physical conservation law. |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Mechanism chain from conditions to behavior or evidence |

| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Key conditions and the strength of effects can change with scale |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman systems without intentional action, power, or institutional positions |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Generalizing local conditions directly into the motives of all subjects |

Safety of expression, evidence coverage, and absence rates at different positions<br>3. Changes in visibility and action taken before and after metrics, scoring, or AI mediation<br>4. Whether the candidate channel effect exceeds the predetermined threshold under the comparison conditions |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Institutions<br>2. Resource allocation<br>3. Platforms<br>4. Metrics<br>5. Relationship networks<br>6. Historical accumulations |

| Normative status (`normative_status`) | Advantages under the conditions or outcomes of systemic selection do not establish normative justification |

R3 also requires evidence of a current carrier, retention across windows, and a sustained effect. Do not extrapolate immediate effects into persistence. Establishing a channel does not establish malice, responsibility, or authorization. |

It does not authorize changing rules or platforms, allocating resources, evaluating subjects, or taking action concerning them. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. Under the same institutional conditions, people at positions with different safety and power take opposite actions<br>2. Using a label such as “power” or “platform” in place of an actual causal channel leaves the state change unexplained |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people at different positions can submit differences in mechanisms, absence signals, and impacts on safety through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original judgment about the potential field or the decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline actually revoke unsupported attribution to a potential field, remove labels on positions and their downstream effects on evaluation and action, and restore the unresolved status. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Personnel<br>2. Positions<br>3. Procedures<br>4. Facilities<br>5. Budgets |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Task assigners<br>2. Resource allocators<br>3. Parties granting authorization<br>4. Parties responsible for oversight<br>5. Parties responsible for remedy |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | High efficiency or the ability to carry a heavy load does not establish normative justification |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | R0 describes tasks, capacity, and recovery within the same window. R1 uses G2 and CM-LOAD to explain a specified channel within stated limits. R3 uses §4.5 to judge cumulative damage or delayed recovery through transmission by a current carrier. R2 judges historical increments beyond the current representation only with additional G3. Persistent correlation, a temporary overload, or an overall average cannot substitute for the corresponding evidence. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of load, capacity, recovery, local overload, and needs to reduce load or add resources. It does not authorize task reduction, resource commitments, personnel action based on performance, or compelling people to bear the load. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. Average capacity overall is adequate, but some key uptake positions remain overloaded<br>2. Metaphors of entropy or resilience alone fail to identify tasks, capacity, or recovery channels |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, people taking up work can report hidden labor, overload, and recovery needs through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original judgment about load or the chain of performance or task decisions |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline revoke erroneous load judgments and their effects on performance or responsibility and actually restore task, resource, and record states. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers individual state events, cases of local groups, populations of intentional collectives, phase distributions, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers sites of action, organizational or institutional boundaries, digital spaces for collaboration, and the reach of evolution across domains. T=Time span covers state windows, transition lags, regression, dormancy, hysteresis, and generational cycles. O=Organizational level covers member roles, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers state events, mechanisms of interaction in transitions, meso-level phase structures, institutionalization, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers original state events, transition sequences, local cases, phase distributions, state metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers direct members and people taking up work, people who exit, indirectly affected people, second-order consequences, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers local phase clusters, connections across levels, splits and mergers, functional transfers, and succession paths. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for naming phases, adopting monitoring, trials, advancement, orderly exit, and elimination. Preserve mixed phases |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | States of human intentional collectives that are taking shape or operating. Components may be present, forming, missing, or inapplicable, as judged under the specific prototype conditions |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Conditions of applicability<br>2. Phase criteria<br>3. Nonlinear paths<br>4. X0 does not count as an eighth stage |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Distribution of local phases<br>2. Transitions across levels<br>3. Continuity of uptake through succession<br>4. Protection and exit |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Those taking up work, institutional carriers, and effective objects can change with the phase |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Populations, purely technical systems, or categories based on similarity when there are no grounds for identifying a human intentional collective. Lack of mature uptake during formation is no basis for categorical exclusion |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Treating the phase of one case as directly representative of the population<br>2. Transferring human stages into the universal core |

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | Prototypes are S0, S1, S2, S3, S4, S5, and S6. Classification modes are `single_dominant`, `mixed`, `parallel`, and `unknown`. X0 is an independent path registered under R4 and is not listed as an eighth state |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Concurrent states of orientation, generation, uptake, institutionalization, and feedback variables<br>2. Records of repeated matches to phase criteria across observation windows<br>3. Trajectories of skipped stages, parallelism, mixing, regression, splitting, merging, and dormancy<br>4. Records of functional transfer, carryover of responsibilities, and orderly exit in X0 |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Phase variables<br>2. Transition records<br>3. Traces<br>4. Uptake and institutional status |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Orientation anchor<br>2. Generative node<br>3. Uptake layer<br>4. Feedback write-back<br>5. Structural load<br>6. Pattern-level phase classification does not require G3. A causal transition separately requires CAUSAL. R5 checks persistence or delayed recovery through a current carrier under §4.5. R3 alone additionally requires G3 to test the extra historical conditional increment. H5 supports only the corresponding candidate traces. |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Phase judgments. 2. Continuity of uptake through succession. 3. Needs for orderly exit and repair |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register phase observation windows, transition lags, regression, and dormancy |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record mixed phases, splits, mergers, and scale differences |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Local groups and roles that an overall phase cannot represent |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. Members<br>2. People taking up the work<br>3. Dissenters<br>4. People who exit<br>5. Successors |

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Collective action<br>2. Organizational structure<br>3. Institutional records<br>4. Shared memory |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Analysts who make the phase judgment<br>2. Decision makers and parties granting authorization for action based on it |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | Phases do not rank health, success, or normative justification |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | Apply each route’s complete conditions separately. R0 classifies only component applicability and missing components. R1 matches S0–S6 prototypes under §8.2–8.3 without requiring CM-PHASE. R2 gives a limited explanation of a candidate causal phase transition under CM-PHASE and G2-instance conditions. R3 explains historical conditional increments under CM-PHASE and G3-instance conditions. R4 independently judges X0 path status under §8.5 without requiring S0–S6 or a match to CM-PHASE. R5 judges mechanisms maintained by a current carrier across windows under CM-PHASE, G2-instance, and §4.5 evidence. Routes cannot supply one another’s missing conditions, and none authorizes action in reality. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | Depending on the selected route, this variable produces component applicability, prototype matches, mixed states, limited explanations of phase mechanisms or historical conditional increments, independent X0 path status, uncertainty, and observation needs. It does not authorize trials, advancement, merger, orderly exit, or elimination. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. The same collective simultaneously exhibits a mixed phase of uptake taking shape in S2 and vulnerabilities accumulating in S5<br>2. Orderly exit X0 preserves functional transfer and does not amount to failure of a stage |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, members can challenge phase evidence, linear assumptions, and uses that impose a moral ranking through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original phase judgment or decision chain |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline revoke the phase designation and its effects on downstream decisions and actually restore variable-level description and unresolved status. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

C. Nine-Axis Scale and Object Contract

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Nine-axis scale profile (`scale_profile`) | SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A=Aggregation level covers individual acts of undertaking, cases involving actors, populations of relationships or organizations, distributions of costs and voluntariness, and aggregation rules. X=Spatial extent covers sites of relationships and care, organizational boundaries, digital public spaces, and the reach of benefits across domains. T=Time span covers windows for immediate action, sustained undertaking, exhaustion, recovery, and effects across generations. O=Organizational level covers roles in action, relationships or teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C=Causal level covers events of undertaking, mechanisms of interaction between actions and costs, meso-level relational structures, institutional arrangements of responsibility, and systemic conditions. R=Resolution covers original actions and costs, sequences of undertaking, cases involving actors, distributions of costs and voluntariness, consequence metrics, and summaries. Record compression loss as well. I=Scope of influence covers direct actors and beneficiaries, dependents, those indirectly taking over, second-order spillovers, and impacts across domains and generations. N=Network topology scope covers dependencies in care, links of benefit, alternative arrangements for taking over, exit paths, and cost networks across domains. J=Scope of jurisdiction and authorization requires separate authorization records for demands to take up work, resource use, refusal, stopping, protection, and remedy. Preserve differences in voluntariness, costs, and exit |
| Effective object (`effective_object`) | Human arrangements for undertaking in which action, cost, direction, voluntariness, conditions for refusal, and consequences must be examined separately. Judge R0, R1, and R2 under their own conditions without first screening actions by their structural consequences |
| Scale invariants (`scale_invariants`) | 1. Costs, voluntariness, direction, alternative explanations, consequences, and rights to stop |
| Required additions for scale elevation (`required_scale_additions`) | 1. Distribution of voluntariness<br>2. Relations of representation<br>3. Cost spillovers<br>4. A genuine means of exit and protection provided through representatives |
| What changes with scale (`changing_semantics`) | 1. Forms of undertaking, positions bearing costs, and beneficiaries can change |
| Inapplicable objects (`non_applicable_objects`) | 1. Nonhuman systems without intentionality, voluntariness, responsibility, or the capability for meaning |
| Prohibited elevations (`forbidden_elevation`) | 1. Elevating individual undertaking to a group duty<br>2. Transferring the concept of human undertaking into the nonhuman core |

D. State, Evidence, and Variable Flows

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| State set (`state`) | 1. Candidate<br>2. Voluntary and bounded<br>3. Risk of coercion<br>4. Unilateral exhaustion<br>5. Stopping or exit |
| Observables (`observables`) | 1. Time and resources invested, opportunities forgone, and bodily and psychological costs of the action<br>2. Whether refusal, exit, stopping, and renegotiation are genuinely available<br>3. Direction of the action, positions receiving benefits, and detectable structural consequences<br>4. Alternative explanations such as coercion, fear, dependence, interests, and acting for show |
| Evidence requirements (`evidence`) | 1. Actions and costs<br>2. Conditions for refusal and exit<br>3. Alternative explanations<br>4. Benefits and consequences |
| Input dependencies and interface content (`input_dependencies`) | 1. Orientation anchor<br>2. Uptake layer<br>3. Conditional potential field<br>4. Power and safety |
| Output effects and variable flows (`output_effects`) | 1. Distribution of costs<br>2. States of relationships and institutions<br>3. Stopping and repair |
| Time window and lag (`time_window_and_lag`) | Register immediate costs, sustained undertaking, exhaustion, and recovery lags |
| Uncertainty (`uncertainty`) | Record dependence, fear, hidden coercion, and safety of expression |
| Local exclusion zone (`local_exclusion_zone`) | Positions where people cannot refuse safely, cannot exit, or are masked by moral pressure |
| Affected positions (`affected_positions`) | 1. Actors<br>2. Beneficiaries<br>3. Dependents<br>4. People taking over |

E. Uptake, Responsibility, Normative Status, Ceilings, and Correction

| Field | What to Record |
| --- | --- |
| Uptake carriers (`carrier`) | 1. Specific actors<br>2. Relational practices<br>3. Arrangements for care or responsibility |
| Responsible parties (`responsible_subject`) | 1. Those making demands<br>2. Parties granting authorization<br>3. Parties responsible as beneficiaries<br>4. Parties responsible for remedy |
| Normative status (`normative_status`) | Subject to N4. It cannot be commanded or conscripted |
| Judgment ceiling (`judgment_ceiling`) | Apply the limits for R0, R1, and R2 separately. R0 describes only costs, direction, risks, and candidate actions. With corresponding evidence, R1 describes the action’s voluntariness and conditions for refusal and exit. R2 judges structural consequences within a limited scope only if the corresponding G2 and causal conditions hold. No level may diagnose personality, prove love or goodness, infer a duty to undertake, or authorize real-world action. Ethical naming is separately subject to the normative premises of GOV-14. Reaching R2 does not satisfy that requirement. |
| Action ceiling (`action_ceiling`) | This variable produces only descriptions of voluntariness, actual costs, direction, alternative explanations, structural consequences, and needs for stopping and protection. It does not authorize protective measures, demands to take up work, requisition of resources, or adjudication of personality. Any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures |
| Counterexamples (`counterexamples`) | 1. One-sided sacrifice that cannot be refused is praised as love or responsibility<br>2. A claim of undertaking lacks evidence of the stated action or cost. Actual failed attempts and unknown outcomes are recorded separately. Their action descriptions must not be categorically withdrawn for lack of structural success |
| Appeal (`appeal`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, actors can refuse to be represented and report coercion, costs, alternative explanations, and restrictions on exit through a safe, accessible channel with anti-retaliation protection. This triggers review independent of the original judgment or chain of demands concerning the undertaking |
| Rollback (`rollback`) | Under `appeal_and_rollback_rule`, once rollback is authorized, the designated responsible party must within the stated deadline revoke the undertaking designation and related demands and actually restore the conditions of refusal and exit and the state of records and resources. Preserve versions and verification of completion |

`scale_profile` is the complete nine-axis record SP=<A,X,T,O,C,R,I,N,J>. A single “micro–macro” label cannot stand in for it. A is aggregation level, which must declare units, population, distribution, and aggregation rules. X covers physical space and digital boundaries. T covers windows, lags, and cycles. O is organizational level across roles, teams, organizations, institutions, and governance ecologies. C is causal level across events, interaction mechanisms, meso-level structures, institutions, and systemic conditions. R is resolution across original events, sequences, individual cases, distributions, metrics, and summaries, with compression loss recorded. I is the scope of direct, indirect, second-order, cross-domain, and intergenerational impacts. N is network topology scope. J is scope of jurisdiction and authorization. Expanding A, X, O, or I does not automatically expand J. Observing more cannot confer greater authority to act.

Recorded identities and the object’s identity criterion belong to K in the object contract. Relationships and channels of effect belong in `input_dependencies`, `carrier`, or the causal contract. Membership and affected positions belong in `affected_positions`, `local_exclusion_zone`, and observation fields. None may substitute for a scale axis. The N scale axis is network topology. Below, “explicit N premises at runtime” refers to N1–N5 in the normative choice layer. The two must not be conflated.

All 11 variables share the same limit on action. The variables themselves produce only descriptions, evidence gaps, and requirements for action. They do not authorize real-world adjustments. Their output ceilings are as follows.

| Variable | Descriptions or Needs This Variable Can Produce |
| --- | --- |
| HV01 | Candidate structural domains, boundary disputes, and needs for more evidence |
| HV02 | Boundary states, interface barriers, exclusion risks, and testing needs |
| HV03 | Candidate anchors, diverse expressions, comparison results, and needs for more evidence |
| HV04 | Candidate classifications for GC, GS, and GE, state transitions, and needs for more evidence |
| HV05 | CV, RS, costs, capacity, rights to stop, gaps in uptake, and needs to reduce load, add resources, or reallocate them |
| HV06 | Chain connectivity, time lags, losses, interruptions, costs, and uptake needs |
| HV07 | Intake, changes to record fields, execution, duration, gaps in write-back, and needs for procedural repair |
| HV08 | Candidate channels through which conditions could have effects, differences across positions, masking of evidence, and needs to reduce risk |
| HV09 | Load, capacity, recovery, local overload, and needs to reduce load or add resources |
| HV10 | Component applicability, prototype matches, and mixed states under the selected route, limited explanations of phase mechanisms or historical conditional increments, independent X0 path status, uncertainty, and observation needs |
| HV11 | Voluntariness, actual costs, direction, alternative explanations, structural consequences, and needs for stopping and protection |

However obvious the need may seem, any real-world adjustment separately requires C12, explicit N premises at runtime, J authorization, and O procedures. The variables must not authorize testing, load reduction, adding resources, repair, protection, attribution of responsibility, exploratory action, advancement, exit arrangements, or other action on their own.

# Appendix B Numbering Scheme and Glossary

The book uses the numbering schemes below. This table serves only as a navigation index. The relevant parts of the main text govern each scheme’s definitions, contracts, and limits on use.

| Prefix | Meaning | Count | Where Defined |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `D0-D3` | Definition layer covering structural objects, basic state terms, effective feedback, and scale transformation | 4 | Part 3 |
| `U01-U11` | Universal structural primitives | 11 | Part 3 |
| `G1-G4` | Empirical question families (specific tests are established through a G-instance) | 4 | Part 4 |
| `CMC1–CMC3` | Common method constraints covering task and representation, evidential status of materials and scope of support, and facts, values, and eligibility to act | 3 | Part 4 and Appendix C |
| `F1-F6` | Conditional formal propositions on task recovery, closure, availability of a common feasible action, preservation under composition, error propagation, and refinement of task families | 6 | Part 4 and Appendix C |
| `E1-E5` | Epistemic constraints | 5 | Part 4 |
| `C1-C12` | Outcome contracts, propositions about mechanisms, and procedural bridges | 12 | Part 4 |
| `A/X/T/O/C/R/I/N/J` | The nine axes of the scale profile SP | 9 | Part 5 |
| `M01-M09` | Scale transformation operators | 9 | Part 5 |
| `CM-*` | Conditional mechanisms (`FEEDBACK`, `LEARNING`, `MAINTENANCE`, `LOAD`, `PHASE`, and `SELECTION`) | 6 | Part 6 |
| `H1-H6` | Human-domain clauses | 6 | Part 7 |
| `GC/GS/GE/CV/RS/IS/IM/IB` | Three sets of strong types that must be kept distinct (generative condition, generative subject, and generative event, uptake carrier and responsible party, and source/mechanism/behavior of destabilization) | 8 | Part 7 |
| `HV01-HV11` | Unified interfaces for human variables (see Appendix A for the full interface cards) | 11 | Part 7 |
| `S0-S6`, `X0` | Human state prototypes and the orderly exit path | 7+1 | Part 8 |
| `IG1-IG7` | The seven gates, the only main workflow | 7 | Part 13 |
| `SJ1-SJ8` | Strong-judgment kit | 8 | Part 13 |
| `L1-L3` | Diagnostic levels | 3 | Part 13 |
| `TOOL-*` | Six formal tools (`EVIDENCE-LEDGER`, `OPEN-ASSERTION`, `CLAIM-VALIDATION`, `FORECAST-REGISTRY`, `STRESS-TEST`, and `AI-BOUNDARY`) | 6 | Part 13 |
| `N1-N5` | Runtime registration of premises for normative choice | 5 | Part 14 |
| `O1-O4` | Procedures for bounded authorization | 4 | Part 14 |
| `PF-1-PF-10` | Interface between normative choice and the choice record | 10 | Part 14 |
| `T0-T4` | Intervention levels | 5 | Part 15 |
| `APP-*` | Application modules (`REL`, `FAM`, `ORG`, `PLT`, `INS`, `OPI`, `SPC`, `EMG`, and `ULS`) | 9 | Part 15 |
| `GOV-01-GOV-24` | Governance clauses | 24 | Part 16 |

Several other symbols recur throughout the book. `S*=<B,X,R,I,T,SP,K>` gives the seven fields of a D0 candidate object contract (boundary, state variables, relations or transition constraints, interface, time window, scale profile, and identity criterion). `N0` is the null model. `K*/F*` are the target identity criterion and the target functional criterion for repair, respectively. `G-instance` and `H-instance` denote specific test instances for empirical question families and human-domain clauses, respectively. The first definition of each in the main text governs its use.

F1–F6 are identifiers for formal propositions. Used on their own, F and F* can denote a dynamic update, a functional criterion, or a repair target according to their local definitions. Use of the same letter does not establish that the object is the same. G3 tests only historical conditional increments beyond the given current representation. When mediated by a carrier, learning, accumulation, sustained effects, and intertemporal reproduction are tested under their respective mechanism contracts. They do not all require positive support from G3.

<a id="part_19"></a>

# Appendix C Common Method Kernel and Cross-Disciplinary Bridging

This appendix lists methodological responsibilities that can be shared when texts interoperate. Each text retains its own authoritative definitions and way of operating. The common kernel requires no shared chapter structure, artifact count, or numbering. It does not make one text independent empirical evidence for another.

## C.1 Method Kernel and Stable References

| Chinese Reference | Content | Where to Find It |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Definition layer | Objects, states, feedback, scales, circles, information status, and recasting | Framework definitions and authoritative definitions in *World Model* |
| Method constraints CMC1–CMC3 | Task and representation, evidential status of materials, and facts and eligibility to act | §4.17 |
| Conditional formal propositions F | Task recovery, closure, common feasible actions, composition of conversions, error, and dynamic refinement | §4.18–4.19 and this appendix |
| Empirical question families G1–G4 | Grouping, channels, historical increments, and representational closure | Instance contracts in Part 4 |
| Outcome and mechanism contracts C1–C12 | Outcome registration, mechanism composition, and bridging to normative procedures | The individual contracts in Part 4 |
| Governance invariants | Freezing, evidence, baselines, authorization, protection, and the life cycle | Existing governance clauses in both texts |

## C.2 Common Method Constraints

CMC1 requires the task, representation, horizon, environment, operations, and error to be declared together. Test static recovery, dynamic closure, and intervention sufficiency separately. CMC2 requires preserving the evidential status of materials, source lineage, inferential premises, and scope of support. Reprocessing material does not count as independent empirical evidence. CMC3 requires facts, value judgments, and eligibility to act to be established separately. See §4.17 for the full formulation.

The three constraints specify responsibilities for using models. They do not constitute three empirical laws of the world. Whether they reduce errors or impose unnecessary burdens in real tasks requires separate evaluation. Compliance with the procedures does not establish the framework’s effectiveness.

## C.3 Shared Governance Invariants

Invariant one. Freeze the record before results and only append afterward.

Invariant two. Lack of support for a positive claim does not amount to support for the null model.

Invariant three. Definitions, contracts, internal teaching examples, and internal simulations cannot supply external empirical support on their own.

Invariant four. The evidential status of materials, proposition role, artifact status, and whether fields are missing are orthogonal dimensions. Keep them separate.

Invariant five. A single artifact cannot automatically satisfy responsibilities for source tracing, modeling, foresight, evaluation, authorization, and execution.

Invariant six. Model complexity must be subject to simplification, removal of unnecessary elements, and review of necessity suited to the task. Claims of predictive or empirical capability require comparison with a simple baseline and independent evaluation material. Formal proofs, interpretation of texts, and normative arguments are checked according to their own tasks. Inapplicable out-of-sample metrics must not be imposed on them.

Invariant seven. Residuals generate only competing candidates. They do not automatically generate variables in reality.

Invariant eight. Capabilities have domains of applicability and validity periods, and are subject to drift, suspension, rollback, and retirement.

Invariant nine. Aggregate results must not erase low-power positions, privacy, non-disclosure for protection, appeal, or exit.

## C.4 Preserving Differences Across Texts

Interoperability should separately check proposition layers, empirical question-family contracts, null conclusions, scale closure, noncommutation tests, and inferential dependencies, as well as information status, artifact systems, typed unknowns, experimental evaluation, evidence of capability, and model life cycles. A complete contract in one text cannot be replaced by a similar name or case in the other. When the correspondence has yet to be established, keep the differences explicit.

The framework’s nine scale operators need not be aligned with the standard operator steps in *World Model*. The framework’s 14-section scale transformation record is also kept separate from the closed artifact system of *World Model*. The two share only the minimum meanings of what is preserved, changed, collapsed, or omitted, new unknowns, source lineage, domain of applicability, residuals, and stopping.

## C.5 A Mathematical Example of Bridging Scales

Consider a microstate x, a coarse-graining map M, microscopic evolution F, and macroscopic evolution F̄. If the difference between M(F\_t(x)) and F̄\_t(M(x)) remains within tolerance ε over the specified initial conditions, parameter domain, error norm, and time interval, one can discuss approximate commutation for that task. If this holds for only one statistic, it cannot be extended to equivalence of the entire state. A proof must also state the order of limits, how the error is controlled, and the interval of validity. A short-time approximation alone cannot establish a long-term conclusion. This is a general formal example and does not claim to give such a theorem for social objects.

Mathematical bridging requires carrying the source object, target object, mapping, scaling law or limit, initial values, retained historical information, error, and failure boundary together. It does not require all models to use the same representation, imply that every transfer across scales causes task-relevant loss, or identify losses from social recasting with physical entropy. Establishing a mathematical proof, showing that a real object meets its premises, and deciding which option to adopt in practice are three separate responsibilities.

Related studies are 05.05 “Gödel, Incompleteness, and Computability,” 05.06 “Effective Theories, Scale Closure, and Coarse-Graining,” and 05.08 “Model Selection, Parsimony, and Expressive Power.” These numbers help locate material. To check a particular mathematical conclusion, return to the assumptions, proofs, and domains of validity in the original works. A bibliography entry cannot substitute for verification.

See Appendix D for the framework’s map of applications by domain. Each domain retains its terminology, measurements, arguments, and professional boundaries. Teaching examples in the map illustrate conditions and the structure of counterexamples. They do not stand in for experiments that have been carried out or actual cases. Topic numbers connect existing research materials and do not rank theoretical importance.

## C.6 F1 Task Recovery and Worst-Case Error

Let X be a nonempty set of source states, π:X→Y a deterministic representation with Y=π(X), and q:X→V the target. For each y∈Y, the preimage X\_y={x:π(x)=y} is called a representation fiber. The decoder may use only y. It must not read the source state, identity labels, or undeclared history behind the scenes.

**Task recovery proposition.** There exists g:Y→V such that q=g∘π if and only if q is constant on each fiber. If it exists, g is unique on the actual image Y.

Proof. If q=g∘π, the same representation is passed to the same function, so it gives the same target value. Conversely, on each nonempty fiber define its common target value to be g(y). This definition is unambiguous and gives g(π(x))=q(x) for every x. Each y has a source state, so any g satisfying the condition must take that common value. This proposition establishes only the existence of a function between sets. Whether it can be computed on an infinite domain by an algorithm that always halts, and whether a finite budget suffices, are separate questions.

**Finite scalar recovery proposition.** Further suppose X is finite, q is real-valued, the decoder can output any real number, and loss is the worst-case absolute error over the entire domain. Let a\_y=min\_{x∈X\_y}q(x) and b\_y=max\_{x∈X\_y}q(x). Then

inf\_g max\_{x∈X}|q(x)−g(π(x))| = (1/2)max\_{y∈Y}(b\_y−a\_y).

Proof. For any output c, the sum of its distances to the two endpoints is at least b\_y−a\_y. At least one distance is therefore at least half the range. With c=(a\_y+b\_y)/2, all target values in the fiber lie between the endpoints, and the maximum distance is exactly half the range. Taking the midpoint in each fiber and then the maximum over all fibers attains the lower bound.

For a nonnegative tolerance ε, a decoder with error at most ε over the entire domain exists if and only if the range of every fiber is at most 2ε. With an infinite state domain, use suprema and check boundedness. Vector values, restricted outputs, average loss, and probability weighting each give a different optimization problem. Half the range cannot serve as a universal score of information loss across domains.

Suppose the target is binary, the loss is zero-one loss, and the finite set of source states is uniformly distributed. Let n\_y0 and n\_y1 be the counts for the two labels in each fiber. The minimum error rate is |X|⁻¹∑\_y min(n\_y0,n\_y1). Only one label can be chosen for each fiber. Choosing the more frequent label attains the smaller of the two error counts. The error under randomized labeling is a convex combination of these two counts and cannot be lower. This expression describes the given distribution. It does not estimate error frequencies in a real population.

## C.7 F4 Composition of Conversions and Actual Execution

Let f:X→Z and h:Z→W, with the final query q already satisfying q=g∘f. The composition h∘f can still recover q if and only if g is constant on every fiber of h restricted to f(X). If q=k∘h∘f, intermediate values corresponding to the same final representation must have the same g value. Conversely, define k by the common g value on each final fiber. The condition concerns only the reachable image f(X). It imposes no constraint on intermediate values without a corresponding source state.

For example, let X={0,1}². The first stage retains both positions, and the second stores only their sum, while the final query still asks for the first position. The stages individually complete the tasks of storing and counting. Their composition merges (0,1) and (1,0), so it cannot answer the final query. Retaining the Boolean value at the first position repairs this problem without recovering all source details. Changing the final query to counting is simply a different task that can succeed.

Representational sufficiency and correct execution must also be tested separately. Suppose the representations of three source states are f=(0,0,1), the required answers are q=(0,1,1), and the actual rule is g\_actual=(0,0). The first two states require different answers for representation 0, establishing that the representation is insufficient. The third state clearly requires 1 for representation 1, but the actual output is 0. This separately witnesses an independent error in the rule. Changing the rule to (0,1) repairs that error without removing the conflict in representation between the first two states. States for which the output is correct do not lose that correctness because of failures elsewhere.

This counterexample does not require every real-world fault to be uniquely classified. If the internal representation, input at the time, or actual rule is unknown, retain the gap. The continued existence of an original document cannot substitute for evidence that the reviewer obtained it in time. Being able to consult it after an error differs from having it available as input before the action.

## C.8 F2 Closure and F5 Error Propagation

Let F:X→X be deterministic one-step dynamics. There exists F̄:Y→Y satisfying π∘F=F̄∘π if and only if π(x)=π(x′) implies π(F(x))=π(F(x′)). The proof follows by replacing the target q in C.6 with π∘F. If the condition holds on the whole domain, then π∘F^t=F̄^t∘π for each nonnegative integer t. The zero-step case is immediate. To go from step t to step t+1, apply the commutation relation once.

If U is the set of permitted actions and each action has a specified F\_u, verify the condition for each permitted action before composing the updates step by step for any action sequence fixed in advance. If action feasibility depends on states that were collapsed, also ensure that a macroscopic action can be executed in all relevant source states in the same fiber, or explicitly limit its availability. If different microscopic implementations are grouped under one macroscopic action, check separately whether they give the same macroscopic result.

In a finite stochastic system, let P(x,x′) be the transition kernel. If the probability sum ∑\_{x′∈B}P(x,x′) for reaching each target block B is the same for every source state in a given block, define the macroscopic transition kernel using that common value. Whatever the conditional distribution of source states within the block given the current macroscopic block and past records, averaging that common value leaves it unchanged. The projected process therefore updates under this macroscopic kernel. If the same macroscopic one-step law is required for all initial distributions, the blockwise equality condition is also necessary. Check it by starting with all mass at each source state in the same block in turn. Retaining only the next-step mean, or obtaining equality by chance for one initial mixture, does not meet this distributional requirement for all initial states.

Approximate closure also needs a horizon. Let d be a metric on Y. Suppose each reachable x satisfies d(π(F(x)),F̄(π(x)))≤ε, and F̄ satisfies d(F̄(y),F̄(y′))≤L d(y,y′) in the region reached. Starting from y\_0=π(x\_0), let e\_t=d(π(x\_t),y\_t). The triangle inequality gives e\_{t+1}≤ε+Le\_t, and the recurrence yields e\_t≤ε∑\_{j=0}^{t−1}L^j. If the per-step error or L changes over time, adapt the recurrence. The fixed-parameter formula must not still be applied.

These propositions state exact closure and error propagation under the conditions for approximate closure, respectively. They cannot determine whether an empirical model meets the statewise bound, whether it leaves the working region, or whether an intervention belongs to the original operation set. Closure under the usual distribution, closure under intervention, and normative usability still have to be established separately.

## C.9 Different Tasks for Two Queues

Consider two ordered queues (q₁,q₂)∈{0,…,30}², giving 961 states in total. The local overload target is max(q₁,q₂)>15. For the sole purpose of computing expected error, make the 961 source states equiprobable. This differs from making the means equiprobable and does not imply that real queues follow a uniform distribution.

If only the mean m=(q₁+q₂)/2 is retained, (0,20) and (10,10) both give m=10, but the overload answers differ. For a sum s from 16 to 30, the same fiber has 2(s−15) overloaded states and 31−s non-overloaded states. The labels of the other fibers are unambiguous. The minimum number of classification errors is therefore ∑\_{s=16}^{20}2(s−15)+∑\_{s=21}^{30}(31−s)=85, and the error rate is 85/961≈8.844953%. This is the exact count on the complete artificial state domain.

The rule “report local overload only if the mean exceeds 15” misclassifies 240 states. The 155 errors above the optimal risk for the representation are due to the choice of rule. Those excess errors cannot all be attributed to information loss inherent in aggregation. If the task changes to recovering the maximum queue length, (0,30) and (15,15), both with mean 15, require answers of 30 and 15 respectively. The optimal real-valued output of 22.5 gives a worst-case error of 7.5. If the output must be an integer, the optimal radius for this fiber becomes 8. Output constraints themselves can therefore change the answer.

Adding the maximum M makes it possible to answer local overload and maximum length exactly, but still does not preserve which position is larger. With exactly two queues, the smaller value is 2m−M. If each position serves at most c tasks per round and no new tasks arrive, then

M′=(M−c)\_+，m′=[(M−c)\_++(2m−M−c)\_+]/2,

where a\_+=max(a,0). Thus (m,M) has dynamic closure under this symmetric model. When capacity is tied to position as (15,10), the states (0,20) and (20,0) with the same representation become (0,10) and (5,0) respectively. The next representations differ, so closure fails. With three queues, (20,20,5) and (20,15,10) both have mean 15 and maximum 20, but their totals after serving up to 15 queued tasks at each position are 10 and 5 respectively. The two-queue result cannot be extrapolated directly.

This example retains both positive and negative results. A total can answer a query about the total exactly, and adding a task-relevant variable can repair some shortcomings. The same addition need not work for interventions tied to position, heterogeneous dynamics, or more dimensions. Recovery of all microscopic information need not be a success condition for every task.

## C.10 The Strict Stability Condition for Feedback With Fixed Delay

Consider x\_{t+1}=x\_t−k x\_{t−d}, where d is a fixed nonnegative integer, k is a fixed real number, and the initial history x\_{−d},…,x\_0 has finite values. The variable x is a signed deviation. The equation has no noise, external input, saturation, or state constraints. The zero solution is asymptotically stable for all initial histories if and only if

0<k<k\_c(d)=2sin[π/(4d+2)].

Expanding the state to (x\_t,x\_{t−1},…,x\_{t−d}) yields a finite-dimensional linear recurrence with characteristic polynomial p(λ,k)=λ^{d+1}−λ^d+k. All characteristic roots lie strictly inside the unit circle if and only if the solution tends to zero for every initial history. The modes associated with repeated roots decay despite their polynomial factors when the root modulus is strictly less than 1.
