Responsibilities for obtaining additional evidence, defaults in cases of absence, and suspension of implementation require specific rules. Organizational conditions can explain harm without automatically absolving individuals. An individual error also does not erase the role of upstream design, resources, and management.

Authority can pass lawfully between steps of a composite procedure. Each individual authorization must still be valid at the step where it applies. Checking each authorization separately does not mean that procedures can never connect. Several invalid fragments also cannot be assembled into valid authorization. Explanation, correcting source data, notification, changing a decision, compensation, and the person concerned receiving it freely are distinct endpoints. If the original record is reliable but the time conversion is wrong, correct the faulty inference and the decisions derived from it. There is no need to delete all reliable material.

Research topics. 21.01 Natural law, positive law, and legal realism. 21.02 Constitutionalism, separation of powers, and institutional checks and balances. 21.03 Rights, authorization, consent, and representation. 21.04 Procedural justice, evidence, and appeals. 21.05 Punishment, prevention, repair, and transitional justice. 21.06 Attribution of responsibility and organizational harm. 21.07 Legal pluralism, customary law, and transnational rules. 21.08 Algorithmic decisions, digital rights, and appealability.

## D.22 Class, Identity, Inequality, and Social Exclusion

In the main text, see §2.5, §5.5.1, §9.7, §10.5–10.8, and §14.4–14.5. Research entries cover 22.01–22.08.

Income, wealth, cash buffers, control over production and management, qualifications, social ties, and standing through recognition are distinct resources. Research on class and strata, stigma and social boundaries, intersectionality, elite reproduction, colonialism and labor, poverty, and mobility points to different possible channels. An identity category alone cannot determine an individual’s circumstances.

Naming a group does not establish a common will. Naming can have real effects through classification, service eligibility, employment, and interaction. Intersectionality calls for examining how several sets of rules jointly exclude a particular position, in addition to overlap among circles or the number of identities. Statistical interactions, intersections among institutions, and lived experience require separate arguments. An improvement in a group average can coexist with continuing exclusion within the group.

Analysis of education and social mobility must distinguish birth cohort, enrollment applications, admission, graduation, and subsequent opportunities. Measured differences in skill do not establish fairness in opportunities to learn. The success of a few people does not represent the openness of the institution as a whole. Conversely, barriers to entry do not erase the value of actual skills. To establish elite control, trace access, agendas, resources, and mechanisms for removal. Educational credentials or occupations alone do not establish it.

In a teaching example, a benefit raises an area’s average income, but people without identity documents are excluded from the statistics and the application process. The average result can remain valid. Claims about coverage must be narrowed, and people who did not enter the process must be listed separately. The time, transport, care, and evidence-gathering costs that poverty brings should be recorded as concrete constraints. They must not become definitive judgments about capability or character. Recognition, redistribution, and participation rights each raise distinct questions.

Resources become opportunities for individuals through conditions of time, use, control, and implementation. Household totals, business assets, or group averages can answer aggregate questions. They cannot directly assign control and welfare to individuals. An identity label can have effects only through a channel by which it actually reaches an employer, service rule, or interaction. A label used only by researchers does not automatically activate those consequences. Relative gaps can shrink while absolute gaps widen. State the comparison objectives separately.

Research topics. 22.01 Class, strata, and the distribution of capital. 22.02 Standing, stigma, and social boundaries. 22.03 Gender, race, ethnicity, and intersectionality. 22.04 Elites, oligarchies, and reproduction of power. 22.05 Colonialism, slavery, and racialized labor. 22.06 Poverty, deprivation, and scarcity. 22.07 Social mobility, education, and inheritance. 22.08 Recognition, redistribution, and life together.

## D.23 Family, Gender, the Body, Dependence, and Care

In the main text, see §9.7–9.8, §10.5–10.8, §14.3, and §15.3.1–15.3.2. Research entries cover 23.01–23.08.

Marriage, kinship, co-residence, intimacy, property rights, care, and statutory responsibilities are distinct. A family’s total resources do not establish that each member can make decisions and use them. Remittances across locations also do not automatically substitute for daily care. Research on patriarchy, social reproduction, feminist traditions, and work and family each retains its own analysis of labor, the body, rules, and experience.

Care burdens include time spent on care as the main activity, simultaneous tasks, planning and arranging, being on call, and effects on sleep and future opportunities. Counting paid working hours alone is insufficient. To assess how career returns depend on continuity at work and flexibility of time, examine job design, employer constraints, public support, and discrimination. Observed pay differences do not automatically establish natural ability or a single cause.

Intimate conflict, ongoing control, and immediate danger are distinct situations. Communication between the parties or joint therapy is not suitable as a blanket response. Safe exit does not require ideal resources to be in place. Arrangements for continuing bounded responsibilities must not force people who have been harmed to remain exposed. A child’s expression, the weight given to their views, consent on a particular matter, and final decision-making authority should be kept separate. Provide support for understanding and expression first. Lack of independent expression must not be used to deny the help needed for it. See §16.12–16.13 for support roles and professional authority.

In a teaching example, an assistive arrangement enables a person who depends on others to participate in decisions, while they still need daily support. Dependence and autonomy can coexist. The ability to help does not make a helper’s obligations unlimited. Preserve experiences of the body, sexuality, illness, aging, and disability, as well as institutional barriers. Medical treatment, improvements in daily life, and administrative eligibility cannot establish one another. Specific professional and legal judgments require their own procedures.

Time saved in one household may depend on additional work by another household, grandparents, or paid caregivers. In evaluating long-term reductions in burden, retain where the burden goes and the risk of double-counting simultaneous activities, being on call, and coordination. Meaningful work can still be difficult to refuse. Kinship, households, residences, and care networks across households have different boundaries. Averages from two valid populations cannot be treated as paired records for spouses.

Research topics. 23.01 Marriage, kinship, and family institutions. 23.02 Patriarchy, gendered divisions of labor, and social reproduction. 23.03 Feminist traditions. 23.04 Intimate relationships, conflict, violence, and exit. 23.05 Children, development, intergenerational power, and autonomy. 23.06 The body, sexuality, norms, and self-determination. 23.07 Disability, illness, aging, and dependence. 23.08 Women’s labor and relations between work and family (Goldin and others).

## D.24 Population, Generations, the Life Course, and Living Across Places

In the main text, see §5.5.1 and 5.5.4, §6.12, §9.2, §10.7, §14.5, and §15.2.5. Research entries cover 24.01–24.08.

Population stocks change through flows such as births, deaths, in-migration, and out-migration. The identity does not supply a policy objective. Crude rates, age-specific rates, aggregate period measures, and actual cohort outcomes are distinct. Net migration can be zero even when many people move in and many move out. Age structure cannot be translated directly into actual care and fiscal burdens for each family or institution. Comparisons across files also require checking what was actually revised. Under the same version number, country tables, aggregates, and scenarios may not all have been updated together (United Nations Population Division, 2026).

Age, period, and cohort satisfy a specific linear relation. More observations cannot by themselves eliminate the resulting linear identification problem. Shocks at particular stages, curvature, and comparisons that make additional assumptions can still be studied. Early experiences may affect later outcomes through current resources and skills. If current conditions fully absorb the historical residual, that does not mean earlier effects never occurred or that change is impossible in the future.

Analysis of migration must distinguish legal status, wishes, actual feasibility, and coercion. Conditions for choice may change over the course of the process. Having signed a contract does not establish continued voluntariness (ILO, see the definition of “voluntariness”). People forced to leave may still have choices in some respects and no feasible alternatives in others. When explaining recruitment, labor, settlement, or return, check the relevant conditions separately for the current question. Members of the same family may face different constraints involving remittances, care, status, and geography. Succession involves knowledge, positions, resources, decision-making authority, and responsibility. Age does not confer authority by itself. A new generation can revise old arrangements. Preserving memory does not require repeating the old way of life.

In a teaching example, membership in an organization remains the same in number, but experienced people leave, new people enter, and care needs grow. Treating the headcount as evidence of “complete continuity” would miss changes in knowledge and burden. The shared goals of population policy do not give it ownership of individual bodies. Having children, not having children, and having conditions for raising children safely should be stated as separate normative questions. Statistical needs do not justify assigning duties to individuals.

The year of a population turning point depends on the question. The first decline, the first year of a continuous decline, the year a sustained decline is confirmed, and natural decrease do not designate the same point in time. When early experience affects later outcomes through current resources, controlling for those mediators changes the estimand. The disappearance of a historical conditional residual does not establish that the earlier experience never mattered. Lower public expenditure does not establish lower needs. Check whether burdens shifted to families and people who provide support from outside the household.

Research topics. 24.01 Demographic transition, fertility, and mortality. 24.02 Age structure, aging, and care. 24.03 Cohort, period, and age effects. 24.04 The life course, critical periods, and cumulative advantage. 24.05 Migrants, refugees, diasporas, and boundaries. 24.06 Generational succession, inheritance, and transmission within organizations. 24.07 Population policy and reproductive justice. 24.08 Dispersal, leaving home, belonging, and living across places.

## D.25 Education, Knowledge Production, the Professions, and Lifelong Learning

In the main text, see §1.8, §6.3, §13, §15.3.3, and §16.16. Research entries cover 25.01–25.08.

Distinguish the aims of education, access to learning, actual knowledge and skills, the interpretation of test results, the award of qualifications, and subsequent opportunities. Specialist instruction and jointly posing questions can coexist. Student participation does not require adopting every proposal. A justified refusal on curricular grounds and the framework’s own `applied` status under GOV-02 belong to different levels. An examination can measure a particular kind of knowledge without thereby establishing that opportunities are fair.

When an assessment is used to determine individual qualifications, checking distributions across groups cannot by itself substitute for evidence about the individual and a review appropriate to the use. Judgments by teachers or experts also need to account for consistency and uncertainty (see OSR, 2021). After correcting a grade record, check whether the correction entered downstream decisions such as admissions in time. Reissuing the grade does not by itself establish that the original opportunity has been restored or that all losses have been made good.

Teaching, family support, and organizational settings work through specific arrangements of time and resources. Sending a notice, activating an account, parents attending a meeting, and improvements in administrative attendance records are distinct from gains in knowledge. Participation can also increase the work of translation, care, and maintenance. The absence of a G3 conditional residual does not rule out learning by ordinary people. Feedback-mediated learning through a carrier and the additional historical increment are each tested under their own contracts.

Trace the stages of knowledge production that matter for the object under study. Examples include setting topics, budgeting, reviewing, practicing, drafting, publishing, and earning returns, as well as making, repairing, editing, translating, and corresponding. There is no requirement that every practice pass through the same gates. Results in submitted papers cannot directly represent research that lacked the resources to produce a draft. Reanalysis of the same data does not constitute independent replication. Professional capabilities, credentials, decision authority, and public trust each require separate evidence. The experience of patients or practitioners can change which questions matter without by itself establishing the overall causal effect.

Teaching example. Trainees acquire new skills, but there are no corresponding jobs locally. This does not establish that the training yielded no learning, and course completion provides no basis for promising employment. Open access for reading, permission to reuse, opportunities to contribute, joint agenda-setting, and recognition of labor are distinct entry points. The absence of records from citizen observers may reflect inadequate coverage. It does not establish that the object is absent locally. Expression and appeal should not depend on prior mastery of the framework’s terminology.

Whether existing skills or qualifications are visible and whether further training is needed are separate questions. Displaying a credential, receiving a response in hiring, securing a job, wages, and net job creation are assessed through separate outcomes. When a correction to a qualification enters admissions criteria in time, it can constitute a real downstream improvement. It does not establish that the final position was secured. Keep the cumulative number established, the number operating in a given year, the number of places set by rule, and the actual number of learners separate. Failure to complete a program does not automatically negate learning in part of it.

Research topics. 25.01 The aims of education (Dewey and other philosophies of education). 25.02 Critical pedagogy (Freire and others). 25.03 Cultural capital, the hidden curriculum, and qualifications. 25.04 Teachers, students, families, and organizational settings. 25.05 Universities, academic communities, and knowledge production. 25.06 Experts, professionalization, and public knowledge. 25.07 Knowledge sharing, citizen science, and open practices. 25.08 Lifelong learning, skill formation, and technological change.

## D.26 Networks, Media, Language, Narrative, and Identification

In the main text, see §5.5.3, §5.10, §9.7, §10–11, §15.3.4, and §15.3.6. Research entries cover 26.01–26.12.

Networks can create opportunities. Language can make experience intelligible, narratives can help people mourn and discuss responsibility, and identity can support cooperation. Analysis should retain these benefits and distinguish ties, actual help, exposure, reading, beliefs, public expression, and action. Weak ties, bridging positions, structural holes, centrality, and resources that can actually be mobilized are distinct concepts.

Teaching example. Each of two groups consists of three people who are all connected within the group. A single edge connects the groups. If people act after receiving a prompt from one neighbor who has already acted, the behavior may spread across the edge. If support from two distinct neighbors is necessary, receiving the message may be insufficient. The cumulative success probability for repeated independent attempts, 1−(1−p)^n, is distinct from social reinforcement. Several social nodes can offer support while sharing the same factual source.

Silence can stem from risk, privacy, lack of exposure, or lack of interest. It cannot automatically be taken as approval or opposition. Being unverified, being demonstrably false, and being deliberately deceptive are distinct states. Strong collective emotion need not be irrational, and actual harm does not make an identity group responsible for all of it. Clear boundaries do not imply that a group is closed, and identity fusion or costly behavior does not automatically establish free consent.

In translation, distinguish words and sentences, the message as a whole, the audience’s task, and the authority to represent others. Language proficiency, listeners’ understanding, institutional access, and the quality of the content each have their own channels. Close reading of narratives can explain experience and meaning without substituting for technical causal analysis. Conversely, identifying a technical cause does not make the work of memory and literature redundant. Facts learned later cannot be treated as facts the narrator already knew on the day of the account.

Keep track of the actual treatment version when studying a platform. Changes to the feed, a ranking weight, content matching, and author feedback cannot share a single treatment label. A fixed recommendation rule can still generate feedback through human responses. Retraining the model is unnecessary for some kinds of feedback. Evaluate changes to system-maintained profiles on their own, and assess private preferences, actual opportunities, and long-term harm separately. Repetition of the same original factual source does not negate the social coordination that multiple supporters may produce.

Research topics. 26.01 Weak ties, structural holes, and network resources (Granovetter and Burt). 26.02 Diffusion, thresholds, and complex contagion, with Granovetter, Watts, and Centola. 26.03 Social identity, self-categorization, and group boundaries (Tajfel and John Turner). 26.04 Agenda-setting, framing, and media selection. 26.05 Silence, spirals, rumors, and misinformation. 26.06 Narrative, memory, metaphor, and public explanation. 26.07 Attention on platforms, recommendation, and public opinion. 26.08 Translation across languages and the transfer of concepts. 26.09 Language learning, language institutions, and inequality in expression. 26.10 Uncertainty–identity and optimal distinctiveness. 26.11 Collective emotion, terror management, and moral panic. 26.12 Identity fusion and costly shared experiences.

## D.27 Aesthetics, Art, Literature, Games, and Everyday Culture

In the main text, see §1.8–1.10, §5.10, §9.7, §10.6, and Appendix A.3 and A.11. Research entries cover 27.01–27.09.

Beauty, liking, status as art, use, popularity, revenue, reasons for public funding, and social effects are distinct. A work’s form, rhythm, genre, and the experience of viewing it can answer questions in their own right. There is no need to establish group coordination or long-term improvements in virtue first. The study of institutions and power also has an independent role. Materials, skills, evaluation, and channels of dissemination affect what can be made and seen.

Analyze collaboration, conventions, and control in cultural production at each stage. Critical approval does not guarantee sales. The liquidation of an organization does not mean the end of works, skills, or movements. In literature, distinguish voice, focalization, characters’ judgments, and actual reader responses. A point of view in a story is not automatically the verdict of the author or of an omniscient narrator. Science fiction thought experiments can show how paths diverge under different conditions. They do not serve as empirical evidence for mechanisms of societies in the universe.

Play can be worthwhile for the experience itself. Seriousness and free play can coexist. Deliberately imposed difficulties, competition, and rules for roles need not be reduced to their ultimate payoffs. Consumption may also involve practicality, durability, aesthetic appeal, shared experience, or the expression of status. Teaching example. Two objects with the same function offer different experiences because of their shapes and how they feel to the touch. This shows that the specified function does not exhaust the aesthetic experience. It does not establish that all functional explanations are invalid.

Fans’ enthusiasm can coexist with maintenance work, commercial control, and internal dissent. An archive’s continued availability online does not establish that the original community remains active or safe. Macro-level indicators cannot exhaust the meaning of everyday eating, solitude, or care. Discuss ownership, custody, research, exhibition, and interpretation of heritage as separate matters. The right to continue changing how one lives also merits separate discussion. Return and authorized loans for exhibition can coexist. Preserving a streetscape or local memory does not automatically restore the relationships of its original residents.

The claims that function determines ratings, that function is sufficient for beauty, and that an aspect of an object has functional beauty are distinct. Different experience ratings for objects with the same function can limit the first claim without by themselves refuting the other two. Close reading and interpretation of meaning can be completed within the scope of those tasks. Claims of long-term behavioral change require evidence from readers. Archival preservation, personal enthusiasm, ties among members, and the safety of the environment can also persist or end on different terms.

Research topics. 27.01 Aesthetic experience, beauty, and judgment. 27.02 Art production, institutions, and cultural industries. 27.03 Literature, narrative ethics, and understanding from multiple perspectives. 27.04 Games, ritual, sports, and play (Huizinga, Caillois, and others). 27.05 Fan communities, subcultures, and shared enthusiasm. 27.06 Consumption, fashion, taste, and identity. 27.07 Thought experiments and science fiction (for example, *The Three-Body Problem*). 27.08 Everyday life, festivals, food, and bodily practices. 27.09 Historical memory, museums, and cultural heritage.

## D.28 Technology, Infrastructure, Platforms, and Artificial Intelligence

In the main text, see §10–11, §13.8–13.11, §15.3.3–15.3.5, and §16.23–16.24. Research entries cover 28.01–28.08.

The material properties of technology, protocols, deployment configurations, institutions, maintenance, and actual use jointly determine specific outcomes. This does not mean that technology and society each account for half. Bandwidth, materials, and interfaces can each impose constraints in their own right. Default configurations and commercial arrangements can also change who is reached. Evaluate compliance with written standards, interoperability between implementations, consistency of business semantics, reliability under stress, and whether people can actually use the technology at their respective levels.

Maintenance depends on spare parts whose quality can be verified, skills, budgets, permissions, and ongoing labor. Several sets of equipment may share one upstream power supply or the same defective software. Numbers alone do not guarantee independent redundancy. Platform matching and flexibility in working hours can bring real benefits. Pricing, eligibility, future opportunities, and appeals can also be centrally controlled. The presence of an algorithm cannot by itself determine all aspects of legal employment status.

For AI, distinguish the attribution of generated content, support in the source text, external facts, and eligibility for the intended use. Retrieval helps obtain material and provides no guarantee of its truth. Internal consistency can help detect one class of errors and can also reproduce errors reliably. Suggestions, tool requests, execution of a procedure, changes to external state, and consequences are five distinct stages. Human oversight requires access to the relevant material, capability, time to review beforehand, and a manageable workload. A rejection signal must actually reach the actuator.

Teaching example. A user clicks Reject after the task in the backend has become irrevocable. Approval in name only does not provide a genuine veto. For privacy, distinguish collection, inference, reuse, retention, and downstream deletion. Issuing a deletion instruction does not establish that deletion has been completed. Assess actual possession of data, permitted use, recognition for accounting purposes, transactions, and eligibility to supply the data across borders separately. A lack of recognition as an asset for accounting purposes does not imply a lack of use or value (see Ministry of Finance, 2023). It also does not lift applicable restrictions on use. When studying technological transitions, track tasks, independent skills, career opportunities, and meaning separately. Tool proficiency and occupational exposure do not stand in for long-term outcomes. Before removing an older support that still serves a necessary function, check the availability of a substitute and the consequences of disruption. Stopping activity and taking protective measures in the face of immediate danger are handled separately under the applicable procedures.

Comparisons of technological necessity must specify what is held fixed. This could be the product name, the binary, the configuration, or the complete deployment including training, permissions, and maintenance. If the later items in that list change, the comparison can no longer be described as a change in institutions alone. The search for alternatives must also hold the task, resources, time limit, and necessary protections constant. Assess attribution of AI-generated content, the existence of bibliographic entries, metadata, source-text support, facts about the world, and permission for the use in question individually. A limited reanalysis or a recalculation using synthetic data can correct a technical quantity. It cannot be represented as evidence that actual users have experienced the corresponding improvement.

Research topics. 28.01 Technological determinism and the social shaping of technology. 28.02 Infrastructure, standards, and maintenance. 28.03 The politics of technology and sociotechnical systems (Winner, STS, and others). 28.04 Platform governance, algorithmic management, and digital labor. 28.05 AI knowledge, generation, and evidence. 28.06 AI agents, automation, and responsibility. 28.07 Data, privacy, surveillance, and digital rights. 28.08 Technological transitions, substitution, and loss of capabilities.

## D.29 Cities, Space, Housing, Rural Areas, and Public Accessibility

In the main text, see §5.5.1–5.5.3, §10, and §15.3.7–15.3.8. Research entries cover 29.01–29.08.

Distinguish the material conditions for urban formation, historical paths, and current agglomeration benefits. Geographic location, administrative jurisdiction, membership, functional networks, and service areas also have distinct boundaries. When the same city ID is used across periods, check the statistical coverage and tracking rules. An unchanged ID does not establish a fixed spatial boundary (United Nations Population Division, 2025). Once connectivity is established on a map, actual access still depends on times of access, capacity, cost, eligibility, support needs, and alternative routes. Centrality does not directly establish power or welfare.

Everyday contact, use at staggered times, and familiarity with a place can support help-seeking and looking out for others. Safety, basic respect, and users’ ability to remain in place still require evidence. Local participation can also exclude renters, future residents, and users from other areas. Observe separately whether a space is visible, whether it can be used lawfully, whether affordable access is continuous, whether people can use it safely and autonomously, and whether people can change the rules under which it is managed.

Distinguish land rights, building rights, tenure security for renters, mortgages, eligibility to return, and maintenance costs. A neighborhood’s name and appearance do not by themselves establish an individual’s poverty, a violation of law, or eligibility for intervention (see Roy, 2005). When combining categories for a specific statistical use, retain the classification mapping and deduplicate the records. Rural areas support production, nonfarm work, care, and supply across places. They are not a less advanced stage of urban development. New roads can expand peripheral markets and can also intensify competition as firms from the center enter. Growing more slowly in relative terms does not amount to absolute decline.

Teaching example. A redeveloped neighborhood has a higher average income, but a different set of residents. The change in the current neighborhood can be real. It cannot stand in for outcomes in the lives of original households or those who moved away. After a disaster, track building safety and renewed exposure, services for households, relationship networks, local memory, feasible choices, and remedies separately. Moving is not necessarily bad, and rebuilding in place is not necessarily good. The observable pattern of partial recovery does not automatically establish a feedback trap.

Using a common list of cities at each time point addresses differences in the sample at that time only. A fixed statistical cohort requires holding the list fixed across all time points. Even a fixed cohort does not ensure that geometric boundaries or residents stay the same. Holding the historical area fixed and holding the current area fixed can serve different valid indexes. State the denominator explicitly. A facility’s service area is a spatial representation based on assumptions. Continuous routes, actual times of access, people’s physical circumstances, and autonomous use still require corresponding evidence.

Research topics. 29.01 Urban formation and agglomeration. 29.02 The production of space and the right to the city (Lefebvre and Harvey). 29.03 Local knowledge and urban diversity (Jacobs and others). 29.04 Housing, land, ground rent, and differentiation. 29.05 Rural society, land institutions, and urban–rural relations. 29.06 Regional development, centers and peripheries, and transportation networks. 29.07 Public space, accessibility, and everyday safety. 29.08 Postdisaster reconstruction, demolition and relocation, and local memory.

## D.30 Ecology, Resources, the Commons, and Sustainable Living

In the main text, see §2.8, §5, §6.9, §12, and §14. Research entries cover 30.01–30.08.

Natural processes, institutional rules, knowledge, markets, and everyday life are connected through specific interfaces. Social choices can change resource use and the distribution of risks. They cannot remove hydrological or material constraints by definition. Resource systems, resource units, appropriators, rules in use, and outcomes call for domain-specific analysis. Forest cover, species, access to fuel, and household life cannot stand in for one another.

Distinguish food stocks, supplies an organization can requisition, and food households actually obtain (see Sen, 1981). In analyzing a food crisis, assess supply, access, delivery of relief, and consequences for nutrition and disease separately. Total stocks in storage or total imports alone do not establish that everyone’s needs have been met.

The costs of exclusion and the subtractability of common-pool resources are distinct from joint ownership under law. Consider rights of access, withdrawal, management, exclusion, and transfer separately. Collective organization and engineering support can complement one another. Local rules are not automatically fair. In energy transitions, distinguish continuous fuel flows, construction activity, in-use stocks, uses of minerals, grades of refined materials, and comparable services. Conservation accounting does not yield a unique allocation of value.

Water withdrawals, net consumption, evapotranspiration, return flows, recharge, and water levels are distinct. Teaching example. The cultivated area expands after field-level efficiency improves. Local water savings need not reduce total net consumption. Better information is also insufficient to guarantee the overall outcome if it does not feed into withdrawal constraints and maintenance practices. Distribution, recognition, procedure, and remedy in environmental justice each require their own evidence. Proximity to a pollution source does not by itself identify individual health effects.

In discussions of climate, distinguish historical contributions, current capabilities, future risks, and adaptation needs. Uncertainty about measurements, mechanisms, scenarios, and normative matters must also be kept separate. Support through cooperation, determinations of legal responsibility, and compensation for specific harms each depend on the applicable sources of law, objects, and conditions. Limits on one mechanism do not automatically rule out other grounds. Scientific assessments do not directly determine shares of compensation or authority to act. See International Court of Justice (2025, §§414–415, 433–435). GDP, material outcomes, living conditions, care, and ecological outcomes need not be combined. Conservation can concern individual nonhuman beings, populations, and ecological processes at the same time. Scientific knowledge, local knowledge, and legal representation involve distinct qualifications. Success in protecting one position does not automatically offset losses at another.

For ecological restoration, state the actual manipulation, the measured endpoint, and the target system separately. If the study directly changes fencing and hydrological infrastructure, the treatment cannot be renamed random reintroduction of predators. Even if only the combination reaches a given threshold, that does not by itself establish a statistically significant interaction. The number of species, biomass, water use, access to food, and people’s lives can change in different directions. Keeping the outcomes separate has more explanatory power than combining them into a health score without a basis.

Research topics. 30.01 Social–ecological systems and human–environment relations. 30.02 Commons, shared resources, and collective governance. 30.03 Energy transitions and material metabolism. 30.04 Agriculture, food, and water resources. 30.05 Environmental justice and the distribution of risk. 30.06 Climate change, adaptation, and intergenerational responsibility. 30.07 Growth, degrowth, and multiple scales of well-being. 30.08 Conservation, local communities, and nonhuman values.

## D.31 Risk, Disasters, Public Health, Command, and Recovery

In the main text, see §6, §8.6, §12.6, §15.3.8, and §16.13. Research entries cover 31.01–31.11.

Hazard sources, exposure, vulnerability, observation, acceptance of risk, and action are distinct. Social conditions cannot erase the intensity of a flood or the presence of a pathogen. Technical estimates alone cannot determine who should bear the risk. Preventive measures should be compared in terms of the harms of action itself, proportionality, feasible support, and provision for review. Uncertainty does not justify an unlimited expansion of authority, and limited protective action need not wait for complete certainty.

Complex coupling, reliable organizational practices, latent lines of defense, system constraints, and routine task adaptations each have their own explanatory scope. A temporary workaround that retains checks is distinct from removing checks. Whether checks have been retained depends on whether the protective function and task-critical distinctions are still tested. The format can differ. A substitute verification procedure appropriate to the task must have a professional basis. The evidentiary value of observing no incidents depends on exposure and observation. A new process may actually have been established after an incident even if its net effect on safety is still undetermined and one branch of feedback-mediated learning still lacks support.

Critical slowing down applies only under the corresponding conditions of dynamics, noise, sampling, and bifurcation. Arbitrary fluctuations cannot be taken as signs of impending collapse. An early warning must reach an endpoint where the warning can be understood, a response can be organized, and protective action can be completed in time. An absence of harm after a warning may mean the measures worked. Infection, testing, confirmed diagnosis, reporting, and hospitalization have different denominators. Changes in beliefs, actually presenting for testing, and health outcomes cannot be inferred from one another.

Medical classification, lived experience, the course of illness, provision of benefits, and treatment choices each carry their own evidentiary burden. Shared decision-making can improve knowledge and does not automatically guarantee long-term health. Emergency command handoffs, coordinated demobilization, the end of special powers, and recovery of living conditions have different clocks. Functional recovery achieved with temporary support is real. A later shock does not necessarily show that the earlier conclusion was entirely wrong. Only recovery claims that exceed the evidence already available need to be narrowed accordingly.

Warning lead time must cover verification, reaching recipients, comprehension, mobilization, and the time it takes for protective measures to take effect. If labels are complete and the target has been fixed, the absence of a post-warning event can still be scored against that target. Estimating how much harm action prevented requires a separate causal comparison. Periods when monitoring is offline cannot be coded as zero events. Recovery of function with temporary power or ongoing support can be genuine recovery. The assessment must retain the support conditions and the population actually covered. It cannot automatically be extended to all pre-disaster users.

Research topics. 31.01 Risk society, prevention, and uncertainty (Ulrich Beck and others). 31.02 Normal accidents and high-reliability organizations (Perrow, Weick, and others). 31.03 Normalization of deviance and organizational silence (Diane Vaughan and others). 31.04 Disaster vulnerability, exposure, and resilience. 31.05 Early warnings, critical slowing down, and response clocks. 31.06 Epidemics, public health, and social trust. 31.07 Medicalization, mental health, and determinants of health. 31.08 Incident investigation, accountability, and collective learning. 31.09 Health care institutions, clinical ethics, and shared decision-making. 31.10 NIMS, ICS, emergency command, and functional degradation. 31.11 Rescue, repair, reconstruction, and false recovery.

## D.32 War, Revolution, Empire, Development, and Historical Paths

In the main text, see §2.4–2.7, §8.1, §10–12, §14, and §16.4. Research entries cover 32.01–32.08.

Identify the state as a legal entity, the government, the military, organizations, and the populations that bear the consequences separately. Realism, institutionalism, and constructivism focus on resource security, the monitoring of commitments, and constituted status. These concerns can support different explanations of the conditions at work. Do not combine them into a single will of the state. Defensive intent, actual effects on security, the other side’s assessment, future capability to honor commitments, and normative justification are also distinct. Communication can reduce a type of uncertainty about information. It does not automatically resolve future incentives or enforcement.

In mobilization, distinguish initial participation, repeated participation, sustained organizing, obtaining concessions, transfer of power, and subsequent living conditions. Supporting without signing up, signing up without taking part, dropping out, and continuing to participate are distinct positions. A later victory does not establish that the outcome was inevitable at the time. Analyses of empire and decolonization retain separate channels for trade, conquest, cultural exchange, and unequal dependence. Formal sovereignty, administrative capability, economic choices, and citizenship rights cannot serve as evidence for one another. Histories centered on leaders cannot stand in for local actors or internal differences.

For historical dynamics, retain Khaldun’s account of cooperation and the reproduction of ruling coalitions, Toynbee’s account of responses to conditions, Tainter’s question about the costs of complexity, and Turchin’s feedback hypothesis. These do not add up to a unified civilizational cycle. Diminishing marginal returns do not imply negative marginal returns, much less negative net returns or imminent collapse. The cost of maintaining a complex model must also be kept distinct from the costs of actual social organization. Retain separate versions for the original prediction, later models, retrospective assessments by the authors, and independent tests.

Modernization, industrialization, and global connections are distinct from one another and do not make up a single development ladder. Actual effects on industry cannot stand in for outcomes for workers, caregivers, or people who cannot move. Peace encompasses at least the cessation of violence, procedures for handling disputes, political arrangements, responsibility, recovery of living conditions, and reconciliation. Cooperation between groups does not establish individual psychological recovery. In a counterfactual, limit changes to nodes for which there is evidence and reassess downstream effects. Retain the conditional roles of historical figures, ordinary people, and implementation at the end of the chain. Do not write the counterfactual as another history whose course is already known.

When comparing historical quantities, first check the target and the range of common support. Preserve disagreements that remain. The total number of violent deaths and the violence-related component of excess deaths overlap substantially and are not identical. Renaming them cannot remove the gap, and the intervals from different studies cannot be stitched together into a common confidence interval. Diagnostic completeness, assumptions used to correct for underreporting in practice, and adjustments for missed interviews belong to different steps. Signing, approval, funding commitments, receipt, settlement, and consequences for people’s lives each require their own evidence. An accurate answer about the documentary record can stand before the overall causal share has been established.

Research topics. 32.01 Realism, liberal institutionalism, and constructivism. 32.02 The origins of war, the security dilemma, and deterrence. 32.03 Revolution, social movements, and collective mobilization. 32.04 Empire, colonialism, decolonization, and state-building. 32.05 The rise and fall of civilizations and historical dynamics (Khaldun, Toynbee, Tainter, Turchin, and others). 32.06 Modernization, industrialization, and globalization. 32.07 Peace, negotiation, reconciliation, and the postwar order. 32.08 Contingency, historical figures, structure, and counterfactual history.

MTUC method, pp. 34–35 of the printed report. [Methodology report](https://population.un.org/wup/assets/Publications/undesa_pd_2025_wup2025_methodological_report.pdf).

Roy, Ananya. 2005. “Urban Informality: Toward an Epistemology of Planning.” *Journal of the American Planning Association* 71(2): 147–158. Pages 148–149 are cited in the text. [Archived copy of the original publication](https://www.wiego.org/wp-content/uploads/2019/09/Urban-Informality-Roy.pdf).

Sen, Amartya. 1981. “Ingredients of Famine Analysis: Availability and Entitlements.” *Quarterly Journal of Economics*, August 1981. Pages 433–435 are cited in the text.

International Court of Justice. 2025. *Obligations of States in respect of Climate Change*, advisory opinion of July 23, 2025, §§414–415, 433–435. United Nations transmittal, A/79/979. [Advisory opinion](https://documents.un.org/api/symbol/access?l=en&s=A%2F79%2F979&t=pdf).

Ravindran, Balaraman, and Andrew G. Barto. 2003. “SMDP Homomorphisms: An Algebraic Approach to Abstraction in Semi-Markov Decision Processes.” Proceedings of IJCAI, 1011–1016. Technical model reference for state and action abstraction. [Paper](https://www.ijcai.org/Proceedings/03/Papers/145.pdf).

Rubenstein, Paul K., Sebastian Weichwald, Stephan Bongers, Joris M. Mooij, Dominik Janzing, Moritz Grosse-Wentrup, and Bernhard Schölkopf. 2017. “Causal Consistency of Structural Equation Models.” Proceedings of UAI, 151–160. Technical model reference for state conversion and preservation of interventions. [Publication page](https://proceedings.mlr.press/r15/rubenstein-17a.html).

Beckers, Sander, and Joseph Y. Halpern. 2019. “Abstracting Causal Models.” Proceedings of the AAAI Conference on Artificial Intelligence 33(1): 2678–2685. Technical model reference on different conditions for causal abstraction. [Publication page](https://ojs.aaai.org/index.php/AAAI/article/view/4117).

Xia, Kevin Muyuan, and Elias Bareinboim. 2025. “Causal Abstraction Inference under Lossy Representations.” Proceedings of ICML, PMLR 267: 68225–68235. Research reference on lossy representations and macro-level interventions. [Publication page](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v267/xia25a.html).

Moore, Edward F. 1956. “Gedanken-Experiments on Sequential Machines.” In Automata Studies, edited by Claude E. Shannon and John McCarthy, 129–153. Princeton University Press. See the discussion of equivalence classes and recursive refinement on pp. 143 and 145–146. [Archived copy of the original text](https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~cdm/resources/Moore1956-gedanken-experiments.pdf).
